Categorical Chern character and braid groups
A. Oblomkov, L. Rozansky

Abstract
. To a braid β ∈ 𝔅 ​ 𝔯 n \beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{n} we associate a complex of sheaves S β S_{\beta} on Hilb n ​ ( ℂ 2 ) \mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2}) such that the previously defined triply graded link homology of the closure L ⁡ ( β ) L(\beta) is isomorphic to the homology of S β S_{\beta} . The construction of S β S_{\beta} relies on the Chern functor CH : MF n st → D ℂ ∗ × ℂ ∗ per ​ ( Hilb n ​ ( ℂ 2 ) ) \mathrm{CH}\colon\mathrm{MF}_{n}^{\mathrm{st}}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2})) defined in the paper together with its adjoint functor HC \mathrm{HC} . We prove a formula for the closure of sufficiently positive elements of the Jucys-Murphy algebra previously conjectured by Gorsky, Negut and Rasmussen.



1.
Introduction

In
[
OR18c
]
we constructed a homomorphism
\Phi
from the braid group
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
to the convolution algebra of

\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-equivariant, where
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
,
matrix factorizations:

(1.1)

\mathrm{MF}^{\textup{st}}=\mathrm{MF}\left(\big(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times V_{G}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\big)^{\mathrm{st}},\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W\right),\quad W=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},

where
\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}=(\mathfrak{n}\times\textup{GL}_{n})/B
is the cotangent vector bundle to the flag variety,
\mu\colon\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\to\mathfrak{gl}_{n}^{*}
is the corresponding generalized moment map and
(\cdots)^{\mathrm{st}}
denotes an open stability condition defined later. The factor
V_{G}
in the definition of the
last space is the tautological representation
\mathbb{C}^{n}
of
\textup{GL}_{n}

The categorical representation of the braid group was used to construct a triply-graded link invariant (homology):

\mathrm{H}(\beta)=\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}(\Phi(\beta),\Phi(1))\otimes\Lambda^{\bullet}V_{G}^{\vee})^{\textup{GL}_{n}},

where
V_{G}^{\vee}
is the
\textup{GL}_{n}
-repsentation dual to
V_{G}

and
\mathbb{H}(\dots)
is the hypercohomology.

The
q
- and
t
-gradings on
\mathrm{H}(\beta)

are weights of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
, while the a-grading is the exterior power in
\Lambda^{\bullet}V^{\vee}_{G}
. It was shown in
[
OR20
]

that the triply-graded homology
discussed in this paper coincide with the original categorification of HOMFLYPT polynomial
[
KR08b
]
,
[
Kho07
]
.

In this paper we construct a pair of functors which we call a Chern functor and a co-Chern functor:

(1.2)

where
\mathrm{Hilb}
is a Hilbert scheme of
n
points on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
, while
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb})=\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb},\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0})
is the derived category
two-periodic
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-equivariant complexes on
\mathrm{Hilb}
.
In a subsequent paper
[
OR18a
]
we put these functors in context of a 2-category of a 3D TQFT (equivariant B-model).
Here we explore the properties of the functors and their interaction with the monoidal
structure
\star
on
\mathrm{MF}^{\textup{st}}
used in the construction of
\Phi
.
We define monoidal structure
(\mathrm{MF}^{\textup{st}},\star)
in section
4.5
and
show the following

Theorem 1.0.1

(Corollary
5.8.3
)
.

There is a monoidal structure
\star
on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb})
such that

•

the functor
\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is a right adjoint of
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}
,

•

the functor
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is monoidal,

•

the image of
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}
commutes with the elements
\Phi(\beta)
,
\beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
.

The monoidal structure
\star
on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb})
is defined in section
5.7
,
and is expected to be a deformation of
\otimes
, see proposition
5.7.2
for the details. The existence of a non-trivial deformations of the tensor
product for sheaves on the holomorphic symplectic variety was predicted by Kapustin-Rozansky in
[
KR10
]
.

Finally, as a manifestation of the categorified Riemann-Roch formula, we obtain a new interpretation for the triply-graded homology as hyper-cohomology of
a particular two-periodic complex of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-equivariant sheaves:

Theorem 1.0.2

(Theorem
6.0.4
)
.

For any
\beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
we have:

\mathrm{H}(\beta)=\mathbb{H}(\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Phi(\beta))\otimes\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathcal{B}))

Moreover, for any
k
we have

\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Phi(\beta\cdot FT^{k}))=\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Phi(\beta))\otimes\det(\mathcal{B})^{k}

where
FT
is the full-twist braid.

In the last theorem we used tautological vector bundle on
\mathcal{B}
on
\mathrm{Hilb}
.
For
I\subset\mathbb{C}[x,y]
,
I\in\mathrm{Hilb}
the fiber
\mathcal{B}_{I}
at
I
is dual to the
quotient
\mathbb{C}[x,y]/I
.

The advantage of this new interpretation is that the Hilbert scheme is smooth, unlike the very singular flag Hilbert scheme
\mathrm{FHilb}_{n}
which
is a homological support of the complex of sheaves
\mathbb{L}(\Phi(\beta))
used in our previous construction of knot homology
[
OR18c
]
. In section
6
we remind the construction of two periodic complex
\mathbb{L}(\Phi(\beta))
of
B
-equivariant coherent sheaves on
the stable part of
\mathfrak{b}\times\mathfrak{n}
, the homology of complex are sheaves supported on the locus of commuting matrices.

As an example, we apply the Chern functor to sufficiently positive Jucys-Murphy braids in
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
and use a combination of the
vanishing higher cohomology of an ’unfolding’ of the resulting complexes of sheaves together with localization formula in order to establish an explicit formula for their link homology. Jucys-Murphy elements of
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
are naturally labeled by
the
n
-tuple of integers
\vec{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
and the sufficiently positive elements have all entries sufficiently positive and sufficiently positive differences
a_{i}-a_{i-1}
,
as explained below.

Recall that the JM subgroup
\mathfrak{JM}_{n}\subset\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
is generated by elementary JM braids

\delta_{i}=\sigma_{i}\sigma_{i+1}\dots\sigma_{n-1}^{2}\dots\sigma_{i+1}\sigma_{i},

and a JM braid can be presented as their product:

\delta^{\vec{a}}:=\prod_{i=2}^{n}\delta^{a_{i}}_{i},\qquad\text{where}\quad\vec{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}.

Let notice that
FT=\delta^{\vec{1}}
,
\vec{1}=(1,\dots,1)
.

Theorem 1.0.3

(Corollary
7.4.1
and section
7.4
)
.

For any
\vec{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
such that

a_{i}\geq a_{j}-1
for any
i,j
such that

i>j
.
there is a integer
M
such that for any

c>M
, if
\vec{f}=\vec{a}+c\vec{1}
,
then

(q,t,a)
-character of the homology of
\delta^{\vec{a}}
is given by the formula

\chi_{a,q,t}\left(\mathrm{H}(\delta^{\vec{a}+c\vec{1}})\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{T}}\prod_{i}\frac{z_{i}^{f_{i}}(1+az_{i}^{-1})}{1-z^{-1}_{i}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\zeta(\frac{z_{i}}{z_{j}}),

where

\zeta(x)=\frac{(1-x)(1-QTx)}{(1-Qx)(1-Tx)},\qquad Q=q^{2},\qquad T=t^{2}/q^{2},

while
\mathbf{T}
is the set of all standard Young tableaux, we denote

z_{i}=Q^{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(i)}T^{\mathbf{l}^{\prime}(i)}
and
\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(i),\mathbf{l}^{\prime}(i)
are co-arm and co-leg of the
i
-labeled square in the standard tableau with
n
squares.

The formula in the theorem was conjectured in
[
GRN20
]
for the Soergel bimodule triply-graded homology,
some variants of this formula were proven in
[
Mel17
]
,
[
Hog17
]
,
[
EH16
]
. Let us also
point out that the relation between the coherent sheaves on the Hilbert scheme of points and the triply-graded homology
was suggested in various forms in
[
ORS18
]
,
[
GORS14
]
,
[
GN15
]
,

[
AS12
]
,
[
GRN20
]
.

Let us also mention
[
GH17
]
where
a link between the sheaves on the isospectral Hilbert schemes and Soergel bimodule triply-graded homology is established.
By pushing forward the sheaf to the usual Hilbert scheme the authors obtain the result analogous to our
theorem
1.0.2
. It worth noting that the construction of
[
GH17
]
results in the localization
formulas like the ones in
[
GRN20
]
only if the parity condition for the homology holds.
Thus it is natural to expect a connection between the results of this paper and the methods of
[
GH17
]
.

Altogether we construct the Chern and co-Chern functors for three braid related categorifies of matrix factorizations: the unframed, the unstable framed and the stable framed category (
1.1
).
We showed in
[
OR18b
]
that the first two categories
provide representations of the affine braid group, while the third one provides a representation of the ordinary braid group, see section
4.2
.

Let us also mention some technical results in this paper. In section
3
we introduce weakly equivariant matrix factorizations from our previous work as well as dg weakly equivariant matrix factorizations. We define tamely equivariant matrix factorizations in this section and show that the push-forward and pull-back functors
preserve tameness. We also construct strictification functor that turns tame dg weakly equivariant matrix factorizations to strictly equivariant matrix factorizations if the action is free. We use the strictification functor to show in section
4.2
that the matrix factorizations which realize the braids are isomorphic to
strongly equivariant matrix factorizations.

In section
2
we recall the basic theory of
equivariant matrix factorizations. In particular, in section
2.2
we discuss various versions of the equivariant matrix factorizations and the relations between the
corresponding categories and in
2.3
we discuss several definition of global matrix factorizations that were previously studied.

In section
3
we remind the main properties of the weakly equivariant matrix factorizations that we used in our previous work.
In particular, in section
3.1
we also remind a construction of the equivariant push-forward in the setting when weak equivariance defined by means of dg Chevalley-Eilenberg algebra. In section
3.3
we study tamely equivariant matrix factorizations introduced in section
3.1

and construct the above mentioned strictification functor.
We also prove a base change statement for matrix matrix factorizations in section
3.5.1
and discuss a Knörrer periodicity in
3.5.1
.

In section
4.1
we remind the construction of the categories of matrix factorizations from our previous work and
in section
4.2
we revisit our previous construction for realization of the braid group inside the category of matrix factorizations.
In section
4.3
define our ‘unframed’ categorify together with its Chern and co-Chern functors
\mathrm{HC}

and
\mathrm{CH}
, proving their properties.

In section
5
we introduce the
framed and stable versions of our category and its Chern and co-Chern functors. In this section
we also discuss the linear Koszul duality relating the category of matrix factorizations to the category of coherent sheaves and
we show that we can combine the linear Koszul duality functor with the (co-) Chern functor to obtain the functor (
1.2
).
In the section
6
we prove theorem
1.0.2
.
Finally, in section
7
we use Chern functor theory to prove theorem
1.0.3
.

4.5.4

2.
Matrix factorizations

2.1.
General facts about matrix factorizations

In this section we recall conventions of matrix factorization theory and
we remind the main properties of the weakly equivariant matrix factorizations that we used in our previous work.

2.1.1.

For an affine algebraic variety
\mathcal{Z}
and a polynomial
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
Orlov
[
Orl04
]
defines a DG category
\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
whose objects
are ‘curved’ homologically
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded free finite rank differential modules

(2.1)

(M,D)=\bigl(\hbox to91.78pt{\vbox to24.88pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 45.88884pt\lower-12.60829pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

That is
M=M_{0}\oplus M_{1}
is equipped with the
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
-linear homomorphism
D_{01},D_{10}
and ”curved” means that the square of the total differential
D=D_{01}+D_{10}
is equal to
F
:
D^{2}=F\mathbbm{1}_{M}
. Let
(M,D),(M^{\prime},D^{\prime})\in\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
then

\phi\in\textup{Hom}^{j}((M,D),(M^{\prime},D^{\prime}))=\oplus_{i=0,1}\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(M_{i},M^{\prime}_{i+j}).

The dg structure of
\textup{Hom}^{*}((M,D),(M^{\prime},D^{\prime}))
is defined by

(2.2)

d\phi=D^{\prime}\phi-\phi D,

Definition 2.1.2
.

The triangulated category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
is defined as
H^{0}(\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F))
.
That
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
has the same objects as
\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
but the morphism space
between
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is defined as
H^{0}(\textup{Hom}^{*}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))

In defining the category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
we did not require smoothness of
\mathcal{Z}
.
In our setting only smooth varieties appear, so we assume smoothness of
\mathcal{Z}
from now on. The smoothness assumption drastically simples the theory of matrix factorization, the general case is discussed in
[
EP15
]
,
[
Orl11
]
.

2.1.3.

Tensor product operation allows us to construct new matrix factorizations.

Definition 2.1.4
.

Given two potentials
F,F^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
we define the tensor product bi-functor:

\otimes:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)\times\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F^{\prime})\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F+F^{\prime})

as
(M,D)\otimes(M^{\prime},D^{\prime})=(M\otimes M^{\prime},D\otimes 1+1\otimes D^{\prime})
. Here we use the usual sign conventions for the tensor product of (curved) complexes
to ensure that the differential squares to
F+F^{\prime}
.

Similarly, one defines the exterior tensor product

\boxtimes:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{1},F_{1})\times\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{2},F_{2})\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{1}\times\mathcal{Z}_{2},F+F^{\prime})

If
F
has a presentation
F=f_{1}g_{1}+\cdots+f_{m}g_{m}
,
f_{i},g_{i}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
, then one defines a Koszul matrix factorization

\begin{bmatrix}f_{1}&g_{1}\\
f_{2}&g_{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
f_{m}&g_{m}\end{bmatrix}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\hbox to106.78pt{\vbox to23.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 53.38887pt\lower-11.03328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Equivalently, the Koszul matrix factorization can be presented with the help of odd variables
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}
: its module is
M=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}]
, while its differential is
D=\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\theta_{i}+g_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{i}}
. Respectively,
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded pieces of
M
are

M_{i}=\oplus_{|S|=i\mbox{ mod }2}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\theta_{S},\quad\theta_{S}=\prod_{s\in S}\theta_{s}.

If
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}
form a regular sequence, then the Koszul matrix factorization is independent of the choice of
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}

[
OR18c
, Lemma 2.2]
up to an isomorphism, so we use an abbreviated notation
\mathrm{K}^{F}(f_{1},\dots,f_{m})\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
. For the independence we need to require
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
to have
finite homological dimension, it is true if
\mathcal{Z}
is smooth.

Let
A,B
be a pair of rectangular matrices,
A
of size
n\times m
and
B
of size
m\times n
, with entries from
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
.
In the main body of the paper we also use notation the following Koszul matrix factorization

[A,B]=[\vec{A},\vec{B^{t}}]\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},\textup{Tr}(AB)).

Here
\vec{A}
,
\vec{B^{t}}
vectors of rank
nm
that consist of entries
n\times m
matrices
A
,
B
,

(\vec{A})_{im+j}=A_{i,j}
,
(\vec{B^{t}})_{im+j}=B_{j,i}
.

2.1.5.

In our work we also use categories of matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},F)
where
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{Z}
is a quasi-affine subvariety of the affine
variety
\mathcal{Z}
. That is
\mathcal{Z}\setminus\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is an affine closed subvariety. In our setting
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is usually a some sort of stable locus.

Since
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is quasi-affine, we have an affine cover
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\cup_{i\in I}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
, here
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{J}=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{j_{1}}\cap\dots\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{j_{m}}
are affine
for any
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{m}\}\subset I
. For dg sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{*}
on
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}

we define

C^{q}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{p})=\oplus_{J,|J|=q}\mathcal{F}_{p}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{J}).

Following
[
LS16
, Section 2.6.3]
and
[
LP13
, Section 2]
we define dg category
\mathbf{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},F)
as follows.

Definition 2.1.6
.

The dg category
\mathbf{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},F)
has the same objects as
\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},F)
but the space of morphisms between
(M,D)
and

(M,D^{\prime})
is defined by

\textup{Hom}^{k}((M,D),(M^{\prime},D^{\prime}))=\oplus_{k=p+q+p^{\prime}}C^{q}(\mathcal{H}om(\widetilde{M_{p}},\widetilde{M^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}})),

where
\widetilde{M_{*}},\widetilde{M^{\prime}_{*}}
are the sheaves associated with the modules
M
,
M^{\prime}
.
The differential on
\textup{Hom}^{*}((M,D),(M^{\prime},D^{\prime}))
is the differential of the total bicomplex:
d_{tot}=d+d_{\check{C}}
where

d_{\check{C}}
is the Čhech differential and
d
is defined by (
2.2
).

Analogously to definition
2.1.2
we define the homotopy category

\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})=H^{0}(\mathbf{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})).

It is shown in
[
LS16
, Corollary 2.51]
that different affine covers
result into the equivalent categories. In particular, if
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is affine then
the category
\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
is equivalent to category

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F})
. Thus from now on we drop subindex
\check{C}mor
since can
assume that all our categories are of the above type.

Remark 2.1.7
.

If apply the construction of
\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},F)
to
\mathcal{Z}
quasi-projective then we obtain a derived category of matrix factorizations
(see
[
LS16
, Proposition 2.50]
and discussion in the appendix).

2.2.
Equivariant matrix factorizations

In this section we remind the main definitions from the theory of equivariant matrix factorizations as in
[
OR18c
]
. We also aim to clarify subtle points of the
construction and explain relations with the
previously developed theory of equivariant matrix factorizations
[
BFK13
]
. We start with an outline of Chevalley-Eilenberg construction and define the category of equivariant matrix factorizations. We compare the equivariant matrix factorizations
with the strictly equivariant matrix factorizations.

2.2.1.

Consider a group
H
such that
\mathrm{Lie}(H)=\mathfrak{h}
,
\mathcal{Z}
is an affine variety with
H
-action and
\chi^{2}
-invariant function
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{H}_{\chi^{2}}
where

\chi\in\hat{H}
is a character.

Definition 2.2.2
.

We define category of strictly

H
-equivariant matrix factorization

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,\chi,F)=\{(M,D)|D(h\cdot m)=h\cdot D(m),D^{2}=F,h\in H,m\in M\}

as category consisting of pairs
(M,D)
where
M
is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
-module with

H
-action and
D
is the
H
-equivariant curved odd (of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
degree one) differential of degree
\chi
. The morphisms and homotopies in this category are assumed to be
H
-equivariant.

Thus defined homotopy category is equivalent to the category of equivariant matrix factorizations was studied in
[
BFK13
, Section 3]
.
In our previous work we used notation
\mathrm{MF}^{str}_{H}(\mathcal{Z},F)
for such categories,
here we adopt notation that is used in
[
BFK13
]
.

In our constructions we use spaces with action of non-reductive groups and we need to define a functorial push-forward along a regular embedding without appealing to the derived category setting. It appears
that such (non-derived) push-forward does not exists in the setting of the strictly equivariant matrix factorizations
[
OR18c
]
. Thus we need to relax the equivariance
constrain and define equivariant matrix factorizations with help of Chevalley-Eilenberg complex (see section
2.2.4
).

Definition 2.2.3
.

Chevalley-Eilenberg complex

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is the complex
(V_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{h}),d)
with
V_{p}(\mathfrak{h})=U(\mathfrak{h})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Lambda^{p}\mathfrak{h}
and differential
d_{ce}
:

d_{ce}(u\otimes x_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge x_{p})=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{i+1}ux_{i}\otimes x_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\hat{x}_{i}\wedge\dots\wedge x_{p}+\\
\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}u\otimes[x_{i},x_{j}]\wedge x_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\hat{x}_{i}\wedge\dots\wedge\hat{x}_{j}\wedge\dots\wedge x_{p},

2.2.4.

In this section we define triangualated category of weakly equivariant matrix factorizations. First we discuss the case when
\mathcal{Z}
is affine.

For a given
\mathfrak{h}
-module
M
the complex
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\overset{\scriptstyle\Delta}{\otimes}M
has terms
U(\mathfrak{h})\otimes\Lambda^{i}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes M
with
\mathfrak{h}
-module structure

x\cdot(u\otimes\omega\otimes m)=x\cdot u\otimes\omega\otimes m,

and the differential of the complex is
d_{ce}=d_{ce}^{1}+d_{ce}^{2}
where:

d_{ce}^{1}=d_{ce}\otimes 1,

d_{ce}^{2}(u\otimes x_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge x_{p})=\sum_{i=1}^{p}u\otimes x_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\hat{x}_{i}\wedge\dots\wedge x_{p}\otimes x_{i}\cdot m

A slight modification of the standard fact that
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is the resolution of the trivial module
[
Wei94
, Theorem 7.2.2]
implies that
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\overset{\scriptstyle\Delta}{\otimes}M
is a free over
U(\mathfrak{h})
resolution of the

\mathfrak{h}
-module
M
.

Definition 2.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be a smooth variety with action of
G
,
H=R_{u}(G)
is the unipotent radical of
G
and
G/H=\bar{G}
is an algebraic torus.
We define a dg category
\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)
,
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{G}_{\chi^{2}}
whose objects are triples
(M,D,\partial)
where

M=M_{0}\oplus M_{1},\quad M_{i}=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes V_{i},\quad V_{i}\in\textup{Mod}_{G},\quad\partial\in\oplus_{i>j}\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(\Lambda^{i}\mathfrak{h}\otimes M,\Lambda^{j}\mathfrak{h}\otimes M)

and
D
is an odd endomorphism

D\in\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(M,M)
such that
D
is an odd (of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-degree one)
G_{red}
-equivariant of degree
\chi
and

D^{2}=F,\quad D_{tot}^{2}=F,\quad D_{tot}=D+d_{ce}+\partial,

where the total differential
D_{tot}
is an endomorphism of
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\overset{\scriptstyle\Delta}{\otimes}M
, that commutes with the
U(\mathfrak{h})
-action and of degree
\chi
. The morphism space between
\mathcal{F}=(M,D,\partial)

and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=(M^{\prime},D^{\prime},\partial^{\prime})
is defined as

\textup{Hom}^{*}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\oplus_{i\geq j}\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(\Lambda^{i}\mathfrak{h}\otimes M,\Lambda^{j}\mathfrak{h}\otimes M^{\prime})

with differential

d(\Psi)=D^{\prime}_{tot}\circ\Psi-\Psi\circ D_{tot}.

In the last definition we define action of
\Psi
and
\partial
on
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\overset{\scriptstyle\Delta}{\otimes}M

by requiring
that this action respects
U(\mathfrak{h})
-action.

As non-equivariant case define the triangulated category
\mathrm{MF}_{H}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)
as homotopy category

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)=H^{0}(\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)).

If
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
is
G
-equivariant affine cover of
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
,

\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\cup_{i\in I}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
then we define the dg category

\mathbf{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},G^{\sim},F)
by combining the constructions from
definitions
2.1.6
and
2.2.5
. The argument of
[
LS16
, Corollary 2.51]
implies that different affine covers result into equivalent triangulated categories
\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},G^{\sim},F)=H^{0}(\mathbf{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},G^{\sim},F))
.
Thus we suppress the subindex
\check{C}mor
from our notations unless we want to emphasis the Cech aspect of the construction.

Let
G=G_{1}\times G_{2}
acts on the quasi-affine
\mathcal{Z}
and
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]_{\chi^{2}}
,
\chi\in\hat{G}
. In this situation one can define
a triangulated category of partially strongly equivariant matrix factorizations

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G_{1}\times G_{2}^{\sim},F).

The objects of this category are two periodic complexes of locally-free sheaves that are strongly equivariant with respect to
G_{1}
and
weakly equivariant with respect to
G_{2}
.

Finally, let us relax the finite rank constraint in our definition and introduce the triangulated categories

\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z},G_{1}\times G_{2}^{\sim},F),\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z},G_{1}\times G_{2}^{\sim},F)

that consist of the matrix factorizations of possibly infinite rank.

2.2.6.
Koszul dual description

The space
\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
has a natural structure of dg algebra with the usual exterior algebra multiplication and
differential defined in terms of the structure constants of the Lie algebra. If
\xi_{i}
,
\xi_{i}^{\vee}
are dual bases
of
\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
then:

d(\xi^{\vee}_{i})=\sum_{k,l}c^{i}_{kl}\xi_{k}^{\vee}\wedge\xi_{l}^{\vee},\quad[\xi_{k},\xi_{l}]=\sum_{i}c^{i}_{kl}\xi_{i}.

Moreover the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is a free dg module over
\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
, the generators
\xi_{i}^{\vee}
act by the contraction.
Thus given an affine space
\mathcal{Z}
with
H
-action the space
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}
acquires the structure
\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
dg module
since
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{h})}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
. The left action of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
commutes with the action of
\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
thus

\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
-module of rank
1
and a free
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
module
is a direct sum of copies of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}
.

Definition 2.2.7
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be a smooth variety with action of
G
and
H=R_{u}(G)
,
G/H=\bar{G}
are the radical and the reductive quotient of
G
.
The dg category
\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\mathrm{dg}},F)
,
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{G}
has objects that are pairs
M,D
where
M
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded free dg
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
-module and
D
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded
\bar{G}
-equivariant dg endomorphism
D\in\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}}(M,M)
such that

D^{2}=F
. The space of morphisms between
\mathcal{F}=(M,D)
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=(M^{\prime},D^{\prime})
is defined as

\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})=\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}}(M,M)^{\bar{G}}

with differential defined by
2.1.2
.

Respectively, we define a triangulated category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\mathrm{dg}},F)=H^{0}(\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\mathrm{dg}},F))
.
Let us point out that a differential
D
of an element
(M,D)\in\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
,
M=\bar{M}\otimes\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}
is of the form

(2.3)

D=\sum_{I}D_{I}\xi_{I}^{\vee},\quad\xi^{\vee}_{I}=\xi_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{i_{k}}^{\vee},\quad D_{I}\in\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(\bar{M},\bar{M})^{\bar{G}}.

In particular,
D_{\emptyset}^{2}=F
and
(\bar{M},D_{\emptyset})\in\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F)
. Moreover,
D_{I}
,
|I|>0
become the correction differentials under functor

{\mathrm{KSZ}}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}=\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}}:\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\mathrm{dg}},F)\to\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F).

Let us define the functor in the opposite direction by

{\mathrm{KSZ}}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{g})}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{g}}

Proposition 2.2.8
.

The functor
\mathrm{KSZ}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}
is fully faithful and
the image of the functor is a subcategory that has as objects matrix factorizations

(M,D,\partial)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)
such that
D^{tot}=D+\partial+d_{ce}
intertwines
the
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h}^{\vee})
-action on
M\otimes\Lambda(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U(\mathfrak{h})
.

Proof.

Given an element
(M,D)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},F,G^{\mathrm{dg}})
locally
M
is a direct sum
of
r
copies of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
. Thus locally the tensor product in the construction of the functor becomes:

\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes U(\mathfrak{g})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes U(\mathfrak{g}).

Here we used simplification
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\otimes_{\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})}\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})=\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Before this simplification the differentials
D\otimes 1
and
1\otimes d_{ce}
mutually compute. Hence
D_{tot}=D+d_{ce}
satisfies equation
D_{tot}^{2}=F

and the triple
(M,D_{\emptyset},\{D_{I}\})
is well-defined element of
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)
. Since
D\otimes 1
and
1\otimes d_{ce}
commute we
we have induced map of the morphism spaces
\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\to\textup{Hom}(\mathrm{KSZ}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{KSZ}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))

Moreover, the differential
D_{tot}
is compatible with the action of
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
. Thus we proved the first statement.

On the other hand
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{g})}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is of infinite rank over
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
. As a vector space it is

\mathbb{B}=\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U(\mathfrak{h})\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h})
and it PBW filtration
F

such that the associate graded space is
\textup{gr}_{F}(\mathbb{B})=\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}]\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Moreover, the associated graded dg structure on this
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h}^{\vee})\otimes\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{h}^{\vee})
module is given by the differential:

gr(D_{B})=\sum_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i}}\otimes\xi_{i}+1\otimes\xi_{i}\otimes\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i}},

where
\xi_{i}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{g}
.

By the usual Koszul duality argument,

(gr(\mathbb{B}),gr(D_{B}))
is homotopic to the diagonal
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
-
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
bimodule
\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
. Since the Chevalley-Eilenberg
differential respects the filtration the homotopy lifts from the graded module

(\mathbb{B},D_{B})\sim\Lambda^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
. Thus we get
\mathrm{KSZ}^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathrm{KSZ}_{\mathfrak{g}}=1
and the statement follows.
∎

Remark 2.2.9
.

Since
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{*}\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is a resolution of
U(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U(\mathfrak{h})

module
U(\mathfrak{h})
in terms of free
U(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U(\mathfrak{h})
-modules, we see that

\mathrm{KSZ}_{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathrm{KSZ}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}=1
. Thus we obtain an equivalence of categories
\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)\simeq\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\mathrm{dg}},F)
and have an inclusion of categories:

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)\subset\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},F)=\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F).

2.3.
Global matrix factorizations

There are several variants of definition of matrix factorizations in the literature. Let us quickly outline the variants and explain the relation with the
category studied in this paper.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be any regular finite dimensional
H
-variety and
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{G}_{\chi}
here
H
is an algebraic group and

\chi\in\hat{H}
is a character. Following
[
BFK13
]
,
[
LP13
]
,
[
EP15
]
,
[
LS16
]
,
[
Hir17a
]
we introduce differential graded (dg) categories

\mathbf{Qcoh}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),\quad\mathbf{coh}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),\quad\mathbf{LFr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),\quad\mathbf{lfr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),\quad\mathbf{Inj}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),

of curved two-periodic
H
-equivariant complexes with differentials of degree
\chi
(
2.1
) where
M_{i}
are quasi-coherent,
coherent, locally free, locally free of finite rank and injective, respectively. The differential graded structure on the space of morphisms is defined by (
2.2
).

If
\mathcal{Z}
is an affine space the dg category
\mathbf{lfr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)
is isomorphic to the dg category
\mathbf{MF}^{str}_{H}(\mathcal{Z},F)=\mathbf{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)
discussed in the section
2.1
and studied in the early papers
[
Eis80
]
,
[
Orl04
]
.

The categories
Z^{0}(\mathbf{Qcoh}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)),Z^{0}(\mathbf{coh}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)),Z^{0}(\mathbf{LFr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)),Z^{0}(\mathbf{lfr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F))
are exact, see for example

[
Hir17b
, Proposition 3.5]
. Let us denote
\mathrm{Acycl}(\mathcal{C})
the smallest thick category that contains all totalizations of the exact sequences of the exact category
\mathcal{C}
. The authors of
[
BFK13
]
,
[
LP13
]
,
[
EP15
]
,
[
LS16
]
,
[
Hir17a
]
in following the construction in
[
Orl11
]

define the derived categories of the above dg categories as Verdier quotients:

\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{C})=H^{0}(\mathcal{C})/\mathrm{Acycl}(\mathcal{C}).

As it is shown
[
BFK13
]
,
[
LP13
]
,
[
EP15
]
,
[
LS16
]
there are equivalences between these categories:

\mathrm{D}(\mathbf{coh}(\mathcal{Z},H,F))\simeq\mathrm{D}(\mathbf{lfr}(\mathcal{Z},H,F))\simeq\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)

H^{0}(\mathbf{Inj}_{G}(\mathcal{Z},F))\simeq D(\mathbf{Lfr}_{G}(\mathcal{Z},F))\simeq D(\mathbf{Qcoh}_{G}(\mathcal{Z},F))\simeq\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},H,F).

In the last statement the categories
\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)
,
\mathrm{MF}_{\check{C}mor}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)
are equivariant versions of the categories from
the remark
2.1.7
. As before we drop subindex
\check{C}mor
for brevity.
Non-equivariant version of the equivalences are shown in
[
LS16
, Theorem 2.9, Proposition 2.50]
, the equivariant version of the equivalences is discussed in section 3 of
[
BFK13
]
(see Corollary 3.13 and Proposition 3.14 there), the most general construction is discussed in
[
EP15
]
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be an
H
-equivariant map.
Following the blue-prints from the theory of quasi-coherent sheaves the above mentioned authors
[
BFK13
, Definition 3.36]
,
[
EP15
, Section 3.5]
,
[
LS16
, Section 2.5.2]
define derived functors:

Rf_{*}:\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},H,f^{*}(W))\to\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Y},H,W),

Lf^{*}:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y},H,W)\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,f^{*}(W)),\quad f^{*}:\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Y},H,W)\to\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},H,f^{*}(W)).

In listed paper the basic properties of these functors are proven.

Proposition 2.3.1
.

[
EP15
]
,
[
BFK13
]
,
[
LS16
]

(1)

There is an adjunction
Lf^{*},Rf_{*}
of triangulated functors.

(2)

If restriction of
f
on the critical locus of
W
is proper then

Rf_{*}
sends
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,f^{*}(W))
to
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y},H,W)

(3)

If
f
is proper and affine then
Rf_{*}=f_{*}
. If
f
is flat then

Lf^{*}=f^{*}

(4)

Rf_{*}(\mathcal{F})\overset{L}{\otimes}\mathcal{E}=Rf_{*}(\mathcal{F}\overset{L}{\otimes}Lf^{*}(\mathcal{E}))

In section
3
we discuss the push-forward and pull-back functors in weakly equivariant setting. For two
H
-varieties
\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Y}
with
H
-invariant potentials
F,W
and an equivariant map
f\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
such
that
f^{*}(W)=F
, there is a pull-back functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y},H,W)\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,F),
since a pull-back of a free module is free.
Moreover, if
f
is a smooth projection or a regular embedding then there is a well-defined
push-forward functor

f_{*}\colon\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H,F)\to\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}(\mathcal{Y},H,W)

which is a right adjoint to
f^{*}
, see
[
OR18c
]
and corollary
3.2.2
below.

Most importantly, in section
3
we define
tame
weakly equivariant matrix factorizations. Under some assumption of freeness of the group action,
proposition
3.3.1
shows equivalence between the category tame weakly equivariant matrix factorizations and strictly equivariant matrix factorizations discussed in this sections. In section
4.2
we remind the
braid realization from
[
OR18c
]
and explain why
all
equivariant
matrix factorizations in
[
OR18c
]
are tame.

In particular, let us also remark that the push-forward of a finite rank module along a smooth map
f:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
with positive dimensional fibers has infinite rank. However, in all cases
considered in our paper we apply non-proper push-forward in the situation when we have properness over the critical locus and we can apply part three of proposition
2.3.1
and proposition
3.3.1
.

3.
Weakly Equivariant matrix factorizations

3.1.
Push-forward: a construction

The main technical advantage of the weakly equivariant matrix factorizations over strictly equivariant matrix factorization is existence of an elementary construction for the regular push-forward
3.1.2
:

(3.1)

j_{*}:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{0},H^{\sim},F|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{\sim},F),

where
\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subset\mathcal{Z}
is defined by the
\mathfrak{h}
-invariant ideal whose generators form a regular sequence
[
OR18c
]
.
The push-forward functor
satisfies the projection formula and the smooth base change, see proposition
3.5.1
and proposition
3.2.1
below.

The construction for
j_{*}
from
[
OR18c
]
relies on an iterative algorithm. Here we explain the algorithm for the push-forward in dg setting. Our previous work relies on the formal properties of
j_{*}
which hold in the dg setting as explain below. In the proposition below we articulate preservation of tameness under the push-forward.

Definition 3.1.1
.

The subcategory
\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)\subset\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
has objects
(M,D,\partial)
with the following property. For any
I

operator
D_{I}
as in (
2.3
) respects some finite filtration of locally-free
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
-modules
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(M)\subset M
,

\mathfrak{F}_{0}(M)=M
,
\mathfrak{F}_{N}(M)=0

\mathfrak{F}_{i}(M)\subset\mathfrak{F}_{i-1}(M)
,
D_{I}(\mathfrak{F}_{i}(M))\subset\mathfrak{F}_{i+1}(M)
and

\mathfrak{F}_{i}(M)/\mathfrak{F}_{i+1}(M)
is locally-free.

To distinguish the push-forward functor from
[
OR18c
]
from the push-forward from the previous section we use notation
j_{\star}
. Later, see
3.3.4
we show that the push-forward coincides with the usual push-forward.

Proposition 3.1.2
.

Suppose
j:\mathcal{Z}_{0}\to\mathcal{Z}
is are regular
H
-equivariant embedding and

F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{G}

Then there is a well-defined functor

j_{\star}:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{0},H^{dg},j^{*}(F))\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{dg},F).

Moreover, this functor preserves tameness:

j_{\star}:\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},H^{dg},j^{*}(F))\to\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},H^{dg},F)

Proof.

In our paper
[
OR18c
, Section 3]
we construct functor
j_{*}
as in
3.1
.
Construction of the functor
j_{\star}
almost verbatim repeats the construction from
[
OR18c
]
but we provide details that are concerned with the tameness condition. In particular, the functor
\mathrm{KSZ}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}
from
2.2.8
intertwines
j_{*}
and
j_{\star}
.

First, we discuss the case when
H
is reductive. Let
\mathcal{F}=(M,D)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{0},H,j^{*}(F))
,
M=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}_{0}]^{n}
be a strongly
H
-equivariant matrix factorization. First, we construct a pair
(\tilde{M},\tilde{D})
,
\tilde{M}=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{n}
that lifts
(M,D)
, that is
\tilde{D}|_{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}=\mathcal{D}
.

A vector space of all lifts of a scalar multiple of
\tilde{D}
is a non-zero vector space and it is
H
-representation. Since
\mathcal{D}
is
H
-invariant
the last
H
-representation has a summand that is a trivial
H
-representation. Thus there is an
H
-invariant lift
\tilde{D}
.

The structure sheaf of
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
has a free
H
-equivariant
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
-resolution

(C_{\bullet},d^{+})
. Since, embedding is regular we have

(3.2)

\textup{Hom}^{<0}_{d^{+}}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})=0.

Next, we observe that
\tilde{D}^{2}-j^{*}(F)
vanishes on
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
hence there is
h^{(-1)}\in\textup{Hom}^{(-1)}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})\otimes\textup{Hom}(\tilde{M},\tilde{M})
such that
[d^{+},h^{(-1)}]=\tilde{D}^{2}-j^{*}(F)
. Since
d^{+}
and
\tilde{D}^{2}-j^{*}(F)
the same argument as before implies that we can choose
h^{(-1)}
to be
H
-equivariant.

Let
D^{(1)}=D+d^{+}-h^{(-1)}
. By the above discussion
(D^{(1)})^{2}-j^{*}(F)\in\textup{Hom}^{<-1}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})^{H}\otimes\textup{Hom}(\tilde{M},\tilde{M})^{H}
. One can show
that last expression commutes
d^{+}
.

We can use
3.2
to construct step by step correction
h^{(-2i-1)}\in\textup{Hom}^{-2i-1}(C_{\bullet}.C_{\bullet})^{H}\otimes\textup{Hom}(\tilde{M},\tilde{M})^{H}
such that

D^{(-2j+1)}=D+d^{+}+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}h^{(-1-2k)}
satisfies

(D^{(-2j+1)})^{2}-j^{*}(F)\in\textup{Hom}^{<-2j}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})^{H}\otimes\textup{Hom}(\tilde{M},\tilde{M})^{H}.

The details of argument can be found in the proof of
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.1]
. Since the complex
C_{\bullet}
is bounded, some large
N
,

D^{(-2N+1)}
satisfies
(D^{(-2N+1)})^{2}=j^{*}(F)
.

It is also shown in
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.2]
that element
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}=(\tilde{M},D^{(-2N+1)})
is unique up to isomorphism. Thus we can define

j_{\star}(\mathcal{F})=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
.

Now we consider the opposite case
H
is a unipotent group. This case was also treated in
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.5]
. But the tameness and dg structure is not discussed there so provide this discussion here. The case of general
H
is a straight-forward combination of the reductive and unipotent case.

As before we start with
\mathcal{F}=(M,D,\delta)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{0},H^{dg},F)
and
(C_{\bullet},d^{+})
being
H
-equivariant resolution of the structure sheaf of
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. By the previous
argument there is
(\tilde{M},\tilde{D})\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},j^{*}(F))
,
\tilde{D}=D+d^{+}+d^{-}
,
d^{-}=\sum_{k=0}h^{(-1-2k)}
that extends

\mathcal{F}
from
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. However, we can not make
d^{-}

H
-equivariant since
H
is not reductive.

Here everywhere below we use short-hand notation
\textup{Hom}^{k}=\textup{Hom}^{k}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})
. Given
A\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}(C_{\bullet},C_{\bullet})
we also use notation
[A]_{k}
,
for the part in
\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{k}
.

Since
(\delta+d_{ce})^{2}=[(\tilde{D}+\delta+d_{ce})^{2}-j^{*}(F)]_{0}
vanishes on
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, there is
\tilde{h}^{(-1)}\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{<\bullet})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{-1}
such that
[\tilde{h}^{(-1)},d^{+}]=(\delta+d_{ce})^{2}
.
Finally, let us observe that since
d_{ce},\delta
are polynomials of

\xi_{i}^{\vee}
as in
2.2.8
, we choose
\tilde{h}^{(-1)}
to be of form

\sum_{i}\phi_{i}\xi_{i}^{\vee}
,
\phi_{i}\in\textup{Hom}^{-1}
.
Let
D^{(0)}=\tilde{D}+\delta+d_{ce}+\tilde{h}^{(-1)}
. By construction

(D^{(0)})^{2}-F\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{<0}
.

As explained, in
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.5]
we can iterate the above process and obtain
h^{(-k)}\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{-k}
such that:

(D^{(k)})^{2}-j^{*}(F)\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{<-k},\quad D^{(k)}=D^{(k-1)}+h^{(-k)}.

Moreover, by the above remark we can assume that
h^{(-k)}
is polynomial in

\xi_{i}^{\vee}
of degree
k
.

Thus for
N
greater that the length of the complex
(C_{\bullet},d^{+})
we get

(D^{(N)})^{2}=j^{*}(F)
hence we can set
j_{\star}(\mathcal{F})=(\tilde{M},\tilde{D},\tilde{\partial})=
where
\tilde{\partial}=\delta+\sum_{k}\tilde{h}^{(-k)}
.

It is shown in
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.6]
that the
different choices of
\tilde{h}^{(k)}
result in isomorphic matrix factorizations. Also in
[
OR18c
, Lemma 3.7]
we show that the iterative process from above can be used to lift morphism between the objects. Thus we obtain a well-defined functor.

To show the last statement of the proposition we observe that the coefficient

[\xi_{I}^{\vee}](\tilde{\partial})
in front of
\xi_{I}^{\vee}
in
\tilde{\partial}
is the sum of

[\xi_{I}^{\vee}](\delta)\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{0},\quad[\xi_{I}^{\vee}](\sum_{k}\tilde{h}^{(-k)})\in\textup{Hom}(\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h},\Lambda^{\bullet}\mathfrak{h})\otimes\textup{Hom}^{<0},

Since
[\xi_{I}^{\vee}](\delta)
is strictly upper-triangular
[\xi_{I}^{\vee}](\tilde{\partial})
is strictly-upper triangular too.

∎

3.2.
Properties of push-forward functors

In this section we show that the push-forward functors
j_{*}
and
j_{\star}
satisfy properties analogous to the properties listed in proposition
2.3.1
.
The key property is the fourth property in
2.3.1
.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

Let
j:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a regular
G
-equivariant embedding then

j_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes j^{*}(\mathcal{G}))=j_{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G},\quad j_{\star}(\mathcal{F}\otimes j^{*}(\mathcal{G}))=j_{\star}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}

for
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{\sim},W),\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y},G^{\sim},W^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},W),\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y},G^{dg},W^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let us discuss the second equation since the first equation has almost the same proof. Let
\mathcal{F}=(M,D_{1},\partial_{1})
and
\mathcal{G}=(N,D_{2},\partial_{2})
.

The pull-back functor
j^{*}
is defined by the restriction of the differentials

j^{*}(\mathcal{G})=(M|_{\mathcal{Z}},D_{2}|_{\mathcal{Z}},\partial_{2}|_{\mathcal{Z}}).

As explained in the proof of proportion
3.1.2
, at the first step of construction
of
j_{\star}(\mathcal{F}\otimes j^{*}(\mathcal{G}))
we need to choose a lift of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
-modules

N\otimes M|_{\mathcal{Z}}
and of the differentials
D_{1},\partial_{1}
,

D_{2}|_{\mathcal{Z}},\partial_{2}|_{\mathcal{Z}}
. Let
\tilde{N}
,
\tilde{D}_{1},\tilde{\partial}_{1}
be the lifts for
\mathcal{F}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{G}
is
a lift of
\mathcal{G}|_{\mathcal{Z}}
.

On the other hand to construct
j_{\star}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}
we need to pick a lift

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
of
\mathcal{F}
. We can choose the lift to be
(\tilde{N},\tilde{D}_{1},\tilde{\partial}_{1})
from the previous paragraph.

To complete construction of
j_{\star}(\mathcal{F}\otimes j^{*}(\mathcal{G}))
and of

j_{\star}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}
we need perform perturbative procedure on the curved complex
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{G}\otimes C_{\bullet}
, here
C_{\bullet}
is a resolution of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
in terms of locally free
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]
-modules.
Since the outcome of the perturbative procedure is defined (up-to an isomorphism)
by the initial data, the statement follows.
∎

By a standard argument we derive

Corollary 3.2.2
.

Let
j:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a regular embedding.
Then pairs functors
j_{*},j^{*}
,
j_{\star},j^{*}
from the precious proposition
are adjunction pairs.

3.3.
Strictification

In the remark
3.3.3
(see below) suggests that in general we can not expect equivalence between the categories
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
and
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G,F)
even if the
G
-action is free. However, the equivariant push-forward used for matrix factorizations in our previous papers
[
OR18c
]
preserves slightly smaller category

\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
which we define above. The main result of this section is a construction of strictification for the elements of
the tame subcategory
\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
under the assumption of free
H=R_{u}(G)
-action.

In this section we relate the tame matrix factorizations to the matrix factorizations on the quotient. First notice that if the action of
G
on
\mathcal{Z}
is free then
there is a natural inclusion functor

\mathbf{I}:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}/G,F)\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F).

The image of functor consists of the equivariant
matrix factorizations with vanishing correcting differentials. It turns out that that
the category of tame equivariant matrix factorizations retracts to the image of
\mathbf{I}

Proposition 3.3.1
.

Let us assume that
G
acts freely on
\mathcal{Z}
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{Z}/G,\mathbb{Q})=0
.
Then there is an equivalence of categories:

\mathbf{Q}:\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}/G,F)

that is
\mathbf{Q}\circ\mathbf{I}\simeq\mathrm{Id}
and there is an invertible natural
transformation:

\mathbf{I}\circ\mathbf{Q}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Id}.

Proof.

Given
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
the space of morphisms

\Psi\in\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is filtered by Chevalley-Eilenberg degree and
we denote by
gr_{i}(\Psi)
the corresponding associated graded piece.

To prove the statement it is enough to construct for each

\mathcal{F}=(M,D,\partial)\in\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)

an element
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=(M,D^{\prime},0)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)
and
an invertible
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}\in\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
such that
gr_{0}(\Psi)
is invertible.
Indeed, we can define
\mathbf{Q}(\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and for
\phi\in\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})
,
\mathbf{Q}(\phi)=\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}^{-1}\circ\phi\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}
. Respectively, the morphisms
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}
define the natural
transformation
\mathbf{I}\circ\mathbf{Q}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Id}
from the statement.

First we construct the element
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}
in the case
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\times G

and the group action is by the left multiplication on last factor. Since
\mathfrak{h}=\textup{Lie}\ (H)

is nilpotent, there is a non-zero element from the center of
\mathfrak{h}
. Let us assume that this element is
\xi_{1}
, that is a first element of the basis of
\mathfrak{h}
used in (
2.3
).

The
H
is nilpotent and we can assume
H=\mathbb{C}^{N}
coordinates
\xi_{i}^{\vee}
and action of
\xi_{1}
on
\mathbb{C}[H]
is the derivative
\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{1}^{\vee}}
.
Since each
D_{I}
is strictly upper-triangular, there are elements
\phi_{I}\in\textup{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}(\bar{M},\bar{M})
, that is matrices with entries in
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
, such that

(3.3)

\frac{\partial\phi_{I}}{\partial\xi_{1}^{\vee}}=D_{I\cup 1}\phi_{I}.

Thus we define
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}=\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{I}\phi_{I}\xi^{\vee}_{I}
. By construction

\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}
is invertible and the total differential
D^{(1)}_{tot}=(\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)})^{-1}D_{tot}\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}
does not contains correction differentials that contain
\xi_{1}^{\vee}
. Hence the corresponding matrix factorization
\mathcal{F}^{(1)}
is naturally an object of category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}^{(1)},(G^{(1)})^{dg},F)
where
\mathcal{Z}^{(1)}=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\times G^{(1)}
,

G^{(1)}=G/H^{(1)}
,
\mathop{Lie}(H^{(1)})=\langle\xi_{1}\rangle
.

By iterating this construction we reduce to the case
R_{u}(G)=\{1\}
where the problem is trivial. To complete our proof we need to cover
\mathcal{Z}/G
by affine
charts
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,M
such that
\mathcal{Z}=\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}\times G
.
For each chart we have element
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}
,
\mathcal{F}_{i}=\mathcal{F}|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}\times G}
as constructed above.

Over the intersections
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{ij}=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{j}
the
elements may differ, that is
\Psi_{ij}=\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{j}}^{-1}
may be non-trivial. However, elements
\psi_{ij}=\Psi_{ij}-1
are constant nilpotent matrices by the uniqueness theorem for the differential equation (
3.3
).

Thus
\psi_{ij}
form cocycle for cohomology group
H^{1}(\mathcal{Z}/G,\mathfrak{h})
. Since

H^{1}(\mathcal{Z}/G,\mathbb{Q})=0
and
\mathfrak{h}
is nilpotent, we have
H^{1}(\mathcal{Z}/G,\mathfrak{h})=0
and

\Psi_{ij}=\Upsilon_{i}\Upsilon_{j}^{-1}
. By twisting
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}
with
\Upsilon_{i}
one obtains a collection
\Psi_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{i}}
which form a Cech
presentation of the desired element
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}
.

∎

Remark 3.3.2
.

In the proposition
the condition of freeness of the action of
G
can be relaxed to the condition of freeness of
H=R_{u}(G)
-action. Then get an equivalence of categories:

\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},G^{dg},F)\simeq\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}/H,\bar{G},F).

Remark 3.3.3
.

This remark was explained to us by Leonid Positselski.
The tameness condition in the proposition is essential. To see that let us consider the case
\mathcal{Z}=U
and
G=U=\mathbb{C}
is a group of unipotent two by two matrices. The group
G
acts freely on the
\mathcal{Z}
by the left multiplication and
we would like to compare
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},U^{dg},0)
with the category of
two periodic complexes of vector spaces
D^{per}(\mathsf{p})
. These categories do not appear to be equivalent, the former is a two-periodic version of
category of
D
-modules on
\mathbb{C}
.

More concretely, if
z
is a coordinate along
\mathcal{Z}
then a generator of
\textup{Lie}\ (U)
acts by
\frac{d}{dz}
. A simplest
object
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},U^{dg},0)
is a triple
\mathcal{F}=(\mathbb{C}[z],\partial,0)
where
\partial=f(z)\in\textup{Hom}(\mathbb{C}[z],\mathbb{C}[z])
. To find the intertwining operator
\Psi_{\mathcal{F}}
from the previous proof we need to solve a differential equation

\psi^{\prime}=f\psi
. This differential equation has a non-zero polynomial solution if and only if
f=0
.

It is clear from our constructions that the strictification functor
\mathbf{Q}
intertwines
pull-back functors.
Moreover the strictification functor relates the push-forward and pull-back functors.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
j:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a regular embedding and the conditions of proposition

3.3.1
hold for
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. Then functor
\mathbf{Q}
intertwines
j_{*}
with
j_{\star}
.

Proof.

The functor
\mathbf{Q}
intertwines
j^{*}:\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Z},H^{dg},F|_{\mathcal{Z}})\to\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathcal{Y},H^{dg},F)
with
j^{*}:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}/H^{,}F|_{\mathcal{Z}})\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Y}/H,F)
. On the other hand the first
j^{*}
is a left adjoint of
j_{\star}

and the second
j^{*}
is a left adjoint of
j_{*}
. Hence the statement follows.
∎

From now on we use notation
j_{*}
for
j_{\star}
. In the cases we are most interested in the conditions of proposition
3.3.1
are satisfied so no confusion can possibly occur.

3.4.
Tensor products and quotients

Suppose
H=H_{1}\times H_{2}
,
\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{h}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{2}
and
H_{i}
acts on
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
.
In this situation we have a well-defined functor:

\boxtimes:\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{1},H^{\bullet}_{1},F_{1})\times\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{2},H_{2}^{\bullet},F_{2})\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{1}\times\mathcal{Z}_{2},H^{\bullet},F_{1}+F_{2}),

(M_{1},D_{1},\partial_{1})\times(M_{2},D_{2},\partial_{2})\mapsto(M_{1}\otimes M_{2},D_{1}\otimes 1+1\otimes D_{2},\partial_{1}\otimes 1+1\otimes\partial_{2}).

Here
\bullet
can be either
\sim
or
dg
. If
\bullet=\emptyset
then
the correction differentials
\partial_{i}
vanish we get a standard definition of the
exterior tensor product from
[
BFK13
]
. Not all matrix
factorizations from the category
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}_{1}\times\mathcal{Z}_{2},H^{\bullet},F_{1}+F_{2})

are of the above form.

Finally, let us discuss the quotient map. The complex
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is a resolution of the trivial
\mathfrak{h}
-module by free modules. Thus the correct derived
version of taking
\mathfrak{h}
-invariant part of the matrix factorization
\mathcal{F}=(M,D,\partial)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{\bullet},F)
,
F\in\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{\mathfrak{h}}
,

\bullet=\sim,dg
is

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F}):=(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(M),D+d_{ce}+\partial)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}//H,F),

where
\mathcal{Z}//H:=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]^{\mathfrak{h}})
and use the general definition for the complex of
\mathfrak{h}
-modules
C_{\bullet}
:

(3.4)

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(C_{\bullet}):=\textup{Hom}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]),C_{\bullet})=\mathrm{RHom}^{*}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}],C_{\bullet}).

The differential of the complex
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}])
inside

\textup{Hom}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]),-)
commutes with the differential
D+d_{ce}+\partial
hence the latter differential descends to

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(M)
.

If the potential
F
vanishes and
\mathcal{F}
is strongly equivariant
then
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F})
is the
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}//H]
-module of (derived) equivariant sections of
\mathcal{F}
. Similarly, in the case of non-vanishing
F
and strongly
equivariant matrix factorization
\mathcal{F}=(M,D)
the Chevalley-Eilenberg matrix factorization

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F})
is homotopy equivalent to a restriction of differential
D
to the
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}//H]
-module
M^{H}
. We use notation

\mathcal{F}^{H}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z}//H,F)

for this restricted matrix factorization. In particular, if the conditions of the proposition
3.3.1
are satisfied then

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{Q}(\mathcal{F}).

The duality functor inverts the sign of the potential and dualizes the underlying module:

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{\bullet},F)\ni\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{\bullet},-F),\quad(M,D,\partial)\to(M^{\vee},D^{\vee},\partial^{\vee}),

where
M^{\vee}=M^{*}
is the
\mathfrak{h}
-module that is dual to
M
and
\bullet=\sim,dg
. The differentials are defined in terms of dual maps:

D^{*}_{i}:M_{i}^{*}\to M_{i+1}^{*},\quad\partial^{*}\in\oplus_{i>j}\textup{Hom}(M_{\bullet}^{*}\otimes\Lambda^{i}\mathfrak{h},M_{\bullet}^{*}\otimes\Lambda^{j}\mathfrak{h}).

Let us decompose the morphism
\partial^{*}
into isotypical components:

\partial^{*}=\sum_{i,j}\partial^{i,j*}_{k,l},\quad\partial_{k}^{i,j*}\in\textup{Hom}(M_{k}^{*}\otimes\Lambda^{i}\mathfrak{h},M^{*}_{l}\otimes\Lambda^{j}\mathfrak{h}).

With these conventions we define

D^{\vee}_{i}=(-1)^{i}D^{*},\quad\partial_{k,l}^{i,j\vee}=(-1)^{(k-l)k}(-1)^{(i-j+1)i}\partial_{k,l}^{i,j*}.

The signs pattern in the above formulas guarantees that the total differential
D^{\vee}+\partial^{\vee}+d_{ce}
squares to
-F
. Also the space
of derived homomorphisms

(3.5)

\mathcal{E}xt(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{G})

is a two-periodic complex of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}//H]
-modules and the even part of its homology is the space of morphisms

\textup{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
. Let us also remark that in the case of trivial
H
and
\mathcal{Z}
is quasi-affine there is an isomorphism between
\mathcal{E}xt(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
and

\mathcal{E}xt(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
, because there is an isomorphism between a free module and its dual.
This symmetry does not hold in the case when
H
is non-trivial.

Finally, let us point out that the space of morphisms between
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
is computed by computing homology:

\textup{Hom}^{*}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=H^{*}(\mathcal{E}xt(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}))

In the equivariant setting (i.e
f
is
H
-equivariant) the push-forward
f_{*}
commutes with
Chevalley-Eilenberg functor.

Proposition 3.4.1
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
be an
H
-equivariant map that is either regular embedding or flat affine then

(3.6)

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{F}))=f_{*}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F}).

for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{MF}^{\infty}_{H}(\mathcal{Z},f^{*}(W))
.

Proof.

By the conditions of the proposition the push-forward
f_{*}
and pull-back
f^{*}

are exact when restricted to an affine chart
\mathcal{Y}_{J}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{J}=f^{-1}(\mathcal{Y}_{J})
,

\mathcal{Z}=\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{Z}_{i}
,
\mathcal{Y}=\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{Y}_{i}
.
Hence
f_{*}
is the left adjoint of the pull-back
f^{*}
,
f^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{J}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{J}}
:

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(f_{*}(\mathcal{F}))=\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}_{J}],f_{*}(\mathcal{F}))=f_{*}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathfrak{h}}(f^{*}(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}_{J}]),\mathcal{F})=f_{*}\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{F}).

Since space of morphism is computed with the Cech complex, the local computation above
implies the statement.
∎

3.5.
Base change

Just as in the case of coherent sheaves we have the smooth base change isomorphism.
In more details, suppose we have affine manifolds
\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y}
and
the corresponding potentials
F,F^{\prime},W,W^{\prime}
that
fit into the commuting diagrams of
H
-equivariant maps of spaces:

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.36096pt}{-25.37451pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{72}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
