\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
\bullet=\sim,dg
.

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let’s assume that the map
f
are either smooth affine projections or regular embeddings.
Then if
g
is a flat map then we have a natural transformation
that identifies the functors:

g^{*}\circ f_{*}\simeq f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g^{\prime*}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}=(M,D,\partial)\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},H^{\bullet},F)
. If
f
is a smooth affine projection then
f_{*}
is an exact functor which is defined by the scalar restriction from
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
to
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]
. Respectively,
g^{*}
is a functor of tensor
product
\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
.

On the other hand
g^{\prime*}
is a functor of the tensor product
\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}]
. The push-forward functor
f^{\prime}_{*}
is a functor of
restriction of scalars from
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}]
to
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
. Since the restriction
turns
\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}]
into
\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
the statement follows.

In the case when
f
is a regular embedding we argue similarly to the projection formula proof, see proposition
3.5.1
. Indeed, to construct
f_{*}(\mathcal{F})
we need to choose a lift
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
of
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{Y}

that is compatible with surjective homomorphism
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]\to\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]
.

Let
C_{\bullet}
be a resolution of the structure sheaf of
f(\mathcal{Z})
in terms of
locally-free
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]
-modules. To complete construction of
f_{*}(\mathcal{F})

we need to run the perturbation computation outlined in the proof of proposition
3.1.2
. The starting point of the perturbation is

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\otimes C_{\bullet}
.

Finally,
g^{*}\circ f_{*}(\mathcal{F}))
is defined as

f_{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
. A key observation is that flatness of
g
implies that if we run the perturbation procedure of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\otimes C_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
then we get the matrix factorization
g^{*}\circ f_{*}(\mathcal{F})
.

On other hand
g^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F})
is defined as
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}]
. Thus to construct
f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F})
we need choose a
lift
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}
of
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}]}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}]
. So we pick

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
as a lift.

Thus to compute

f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F})
we need to perform the perturbation procedure from proposition
3.1.2
with the starting object
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]}C^{\prime}_{\bullet}
. Here
C^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is a resolution of the structure sheaf of
f^{\prime}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})
in terms of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]
locally-free sheaves.
Finally, let us observe that the complexes
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{Y}]}C_{\bullet}

and
C^{\prime}_{\bullet}
are homotopy equivalent. The uniqueness of the outcome of the perturbation procedure implies the statement.

∎

3.6.
Knorrer periodicity

The equivariant version of Knorrer periodicity
[
Kno87
]
,
[
OR18c
]
is used in the arguments of the current paper.
In the equivariant setting the equivalence is studied in
[
Hir17b
]
.

We
include the statement for convince of reader. Let
\mathcal{X}
be a quasi-projective
manifold with
G
-action where
G
is reductive. Let
E
be a
G
-equivariant
vector bundle and
s\in\Gamma(X,E^{\vee})
a regular section. If
\chi\in\hat{G}
is
a character of
G
then
E(\chi)
is the
\chi
-equivariant twist of
E
. In this setting
s
defines a function
Q_{s}\in\mathbb{C}[E]_{\chi}^{G}
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset E(\chi)
be an zero locus of
s
and
i:E(\chi)|_{\mathcal{Z}}\to E(\chi)
is the natural inclusion.
Respectively,
p:E(\chi)|_{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathcal{Z}
is the natural projection.

Proposition 3.6.1
.

[
Hir17b
, Theorem 1.2]

Let
W:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
be
\chi
-semi-invariant function, such that

W|_{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is flat. Then there is an equivalence:

\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{Z},G,\chi,W|_{\mathcal{Z}})\to\mathrm{MF}(E(\chi),\chi,p^{*}W+Q_{s}).

4.
Chern functor

4.1.
Main categories: equivariant structures

In this subsection we introduce the main examples of the equivariant matrix factorizations that are used in our work. We concentrate on the details of
the equivariant structures and of the categories. Our categories have natural monoidal structure and it is defined in the section
4.5

where we discuss the properties of the Chern functor.

Denote
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
,
B\subset G
being its Borel subgroup (of upper-triangular matrices).
Also
T\subset B
is the diagonal torus
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{n}
and
B=T\ltimes U
.
Here
U
is the group of the unipotent upper-triangular matrices and
\mathrm{Lie}(U)=\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{gl}_{n}
.

Recall that the flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}
is a quotient:
\mathrm{Fl}=G/B
and similarly its cotangent bundle
\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}
is a quotient:

\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}=(G\times\mathfrak{n})/B.

A bilinear pairing
(X,Y)\mapsto\textup{Tr}(XY)
provides a
\mathfrak{g}
-invariant isomorphism between
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.
By combining this isomorphism with the
G
-action moment map we obtain
the map
\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}
,
\mu(g,Y)=\mathrm{Ad}_{g}Y
, where
(g,Y)\in G\times\mathfrak{n}
.

Definition 4.1.1
.

The main (unframed) monoidal category in
[
OR18c
]
is the category of equivariant matrix factorizations

(4.1)

\mathrm{MF}=\mathrm{MF}^{tame}(\mathscr{X},G\times B^{dg}\times B^{dg}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W),

where

\mathscr{X}:=\mathfrak{g}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})\times(G\times\mathfrak{n}),\quad W(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2})=\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}Y_{1}-\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}Y_{2}))

and the action of an element
(h,b_{1},b_{2})\in G\times B^{2}
on
\mathscr{X}
is

(4.2)

(h;b_{1},b_{2})\cdot(X;g_{1},Y_{1};g_{2},Y_{2})=(\mathrm{Ad}_{h}X;hg_{1}b_{1}^{-1},\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{1}}Y_{1};hg_{2}b_{2}^{-1},\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{2}}Y_{2}).

Since
\mathscr{X}/B^{2}=\mathfrak{g}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}

by proposition
3.3.1
the category
\mathrm{MF}
is equivalent to

\mathrm{MF}\left(\mathfrak{g}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl},\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\right)
from the introduction.

The elements of
\mathrm{MF}
are strongly equivariant with respect to
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
action on the ambient space

(4.3)

(\lambda,\mu)\cdot(X;g_{1},Y_{1};g_{2},Y_{2})=(\lambda^{2}X;g_{1},\lambda^{-2}\mu^{2}Y_{1};g_{2},\lambda^{-2}\mu^{2}Y_{2}).

The action of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
translates into
q,t
-grading by the rule:

\mbox{for a function }f\mbox{ such that }(\lambda,\mu)\cdot f=\lambda^{a}\mu^{b}f,\quad\deg(f)=q^{a}t^{b}.

One can observe that the potential
W
has weight
t^{2}
with respect to the action of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
. Thus we use the following definition of

\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-equivariance of a matrix factorization
(M,D,\partial)
:

(4.4)

\mbox{the differentials }D,\partial\mbox{ are of degree }q^{0}t.

Remark 4.1.2
.

The standard homological algebra convention is that the differentials preserve the equivariant degree. It is different from our convention since
we assume that the differentials raise the
t
-degree by
1
: the usual convention can not be used in our case since we work with the complexes curved
by the potential of
t
-degree
2
.
In particular, since
qt
weights of functions are even, to satisfy the condition (
4.4
) we need to shift standard
qt
-grading by, possibly odd,
t
-degree.

Furthermore, the elements of the category
\mathrm{MF}
are strongly equivariant with respect to the tori
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
,
T^{2}\subset B^{2}
and
G
and weakly equivariant with respect to

U^{2}
. That is for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{MF}
the assumption

(4.5)

\mathcal{F}\mbox{ is strongly }\mathbb{T}_{q,t}\times G\times T^{2}\mbox{-equivariant and is weakly }U^{2}\mbox{-equivariant}

The first part of assumption the (
4.5
) holds for every category of matrix factorizations that we use in this paper. The matrix factorizations used in
our paper are on the spaces with the natural action of the group
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}\times G\times B^{l}
where
l
can be
0,1,2
. We assume weak
equivariance for the action of
U^{l}
and the strong equivariance of all reductive
quotients.

Definition 4.1.3
.

The Drinfeld center category is defined as:

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{C},G\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W_{\mathrm{Dr}}),\quad\mathscr{C}=\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{g},\quad W_{\mathrm{Dr}}(Z,g,X)=\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{g}Z-Z)),

the group
G
acting on components of
\mathscr{C}
by conjugation:

h\cdot(Z,g,X)=(\textup{Ad}_{h}(Z),hgh^{-1},\textup{Ad}_{h}(X))

In particular, the first part of the assumption (
4.5
) is true for
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.
The group
B
does not act on
\mathscr{C}
thus the category consists of strongly equivariant matrix factorizations. Also the action of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}

is given by

(4.6)

(\lambda,\mu)\cdot(Z,g,X)=(\lambda^{-2}\mu^{2}Z,g,\lambda^{2}X),\quad\deg(\lambda)=q,\quad\deg(\mu)=t.

We assume the grading convention (
4.4
) holds for the matrix factorizations from
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.

4.2.
Braid realization

In our previous papers
[
OR18c
]
,
[
OR18b
]
we worked with a bigger category,
\mathrm{MF}\subset\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}

\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X},G\times B^{\sim}\times B^{\sim}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W).

In particular, we constructed in
[
OR18c
]
a monoidal functor, see definition
4.5.1
for the precise formulas for convolution:

\mathfrak{Br}_{n}^{aff}\to\mathrm{MF}_{\sim},\quad\beta\mapsto\mathcal{C}_{\beta}

It could be seen from the constructions in the last mentioned paper that the elements

\mathcal{C}_{\beta}
are isomorphic to the objects in the smaller category

Proposition 4.2.1
.

For any
\beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{n}^{aff}
the object
\mathcal{C}_{\beta}
is isomorphic to an object in

\mathrm{MF}
.

Proof.

In
[
OR18c
]
we construct an element
\mathcal{C}_{\beta}
as
a convolution
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{1}}^{(i_{1})}\star\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{2}}^{(i_{2})}\star\dots\star\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
where
\beta=\sigma_{i_{1}}^{\epsilon_{1}}\cdot\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\epsilon_{2}}\cdot\dots\cdot\sigma_{i_{l}}^{\epsilon_{l}}
is a presentation of
\beta
in terms of elementary generators of the braid group.

First, we observe that in the case
n=2
,
\mathcal{C}_{\pm}^{(1)}
are strongly equivariant matrix factorizations, see exact description in
[
OR18c
, section 9.3]
. Thus, two-strand generators are tame.

For a general
n
the generators
\mathcal{C}_{\pm}^{(i)}
are obtained by application of
the induction functors to
\mathcal{C}_{\pm}^{(1)}
, see
[
OR18c
, section 6]
. However, the induction functors are compositions of pull-backs along some smooth projections and push-forwards along some regular embeddings. Thus tameness is preserved and
\mathcal{C}_{\pm}^{(i)}\in\mathrm{MF}
.

Finally, we notice the convolution operation, see (
5.1
), is a composition of some smooth pull-back and smooth push-forward. Thus the convolution preserves tameness and the proposition follows from proposition
3.1.2
.
∎

The stable version of the above category
\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}
was used in
[
OR18c
]
to construct a HOMFLYPT
link homology.

Definition 4.2.2
.

The stable version of category
\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}
is defined by:

\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}^{st}:=\mathrm{MF}((\mathscr{X}\times V_{G})^{st},G\times B^{\sim}\times B^{\sim}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W),

where
V_{G}
is
\mathbb{C}^{n}
that is a tautological representation of
G
and

j_{st}:(\mathscr{X}\times V_{G})^{st}\to(\mathscr{X}\times V_{G})
is defined by the condition (
5.2
).

In particular, we construct
[
OR18c
]
a monoidal functor
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}\to\mathrm{MF}_{\sim}^{st}
. The same argument as in the previous proposition shows the elements in the image of the previous
functor are isomorphic to some elements of the stable version of category
\mathrm{MF}
:

Definition 4.2.3
.

The stable version of category
\mathrm{MF}
is defined by:

\mathrm{MF}^{st}:=\mathrm{MF}^{tame}((\mathscr{X}\times V_{G})^{st},G\times B^{dg}\times B^{dg}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W),

where
V_{G}
as in the previous definition.

The proposition
3.3.1
implies the equivalences of categories:

\mathrm{MF}\simeq\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl},\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W),

\mathrm{MF}^{st}\simeq\mathrm{MF}((\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl})^{st},\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W).

Since the
B^{2}
-action on
(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2}
is free we also have equivalences of categories:

(4.7)

\mathrm{MF}\simeq\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2},\textup{GL}_{n}\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W),

(4.8)

\mathrm{MF}^{st}\simeq\mathrm{MF}((\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2})^{st},\textup{GL}_{n}\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W).

It is shown in
[
OR18b
]
, the pull-back
j^{*}_{st}
categorifies
the restriction homomorphism
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}^{aff}\to\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
. Using proposition
3.3.1
we derive a version of main result of
[
OR18b
]
that uses the global matrix factorization studied previously in the literature (see section
2.2
):

Corollary 4.2.4
.

There are monoidal functors
\Phi^{aff}:\mathfrak{Br}_{n}^{aff}\to\mathrm{MF}
,
\Phi:\mathfrak{Br}_{n}\to\mathrm{MF}^{st}
such that

j^{*}_{st}\circ\Phi^{aff}=\Phi\circ\mathrm{for},

where
\for:\mathfrak{Br}_{n}^{aff}\to\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
is a forgetful functor.

Finally, let us state global matrix factorization version of the construction of the knot homology from
[
OR18c
]
. Let
(\mathrm{T}^{*})^{\oplus 2}\mathrm{Fl}
be a double tangent bundle of
\mathrm{Fl}
. Respectively,
j_{e}:(\mathrm{T}^{*})^{\oplus 2}\mathrm{Fl}\to\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}
is the diagonal embedding. For a given

\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{MF}^{st}
we define:

\mathbb{L}(\mathcal{C})=j^{*}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathrm{MF}((\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}\times(\mathrm{T}^{*})^{\oplus 2}\mathrm{Fl})^{st},\textup{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})=\mathrm{MF}_{cyc}

The potential in the definition of the category
\mathrm{MF}_{cyc}
is linear along the cotangent direction and we can apply the Knörrer periodicity to this category and then we obtain the category
\mathrm{MF}(\widetilde{\mathrm{FHilb}}_{y=0}^{\mathrm{free}},B\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},0)
that is used in the section
6
.

Corollary 4.2.5
.

For any
\beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
the hyper-cohomology

\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(\beta)=\mathbb{H}(\mathbb{L}(\Phi(\beta))\otimes\Lambda^{*}(V_{G}^{\vee})^{G})

is a triply-graded vector space that categorifies the HOMFLYPT polynomial of the closure of
\beta
.

4.3.
Construction of the Chern functor: motivation

The discussion in this
section means to provide a geometric intuition behind our construction and the technical
details are postponed till the next subsection.
The Chern character functor

\mathrm{CH}\colon\mathrm{MF}\rightarrow\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}

is a Fourier-Mukai transform going through the category of
(G\times B_{\Delta})
-equivariant matrix factorizations
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathbb{T}_{q,t}}_{G\times B_{\Delta}}(\mathscr{X}_{\Delta};W_{\Delta})
, where

\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}=\mathfrak{g}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n}_{\Delta})\times G,\qquad W_{\Delta}(X;g,Y;h)=\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{gh}Y-\textup{Ad}_{h}Y)),

\mathfrak{n}_{\Delta}=\mathfrak{n}
is (identified with) the diagonal in the factor
\mathfrak{n}\times\mathfrak{n}
of the space
\mathscr{X}
, the group
B_{\Delta}=B
is (identified with) the diagonal in
B^{2}
acting on that space and the action of
G\times B_{\Delta}
on
\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}
is

(h,b)\cdot(X;g_{1},Y;g)=(\mathrm{Ad}_{h}X;hg_{1}b^{-1},\mathrm{Ad}_{b}Y;\mathrm{Ad}_{h}g)

We define two equivariant maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}}

explicitly as

(4.9)

\hat{f}_{\Delta}(X;g,Y;h)=(X;gh,Y;h,Y),\qquad\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(X;g,Y;h)=(\mathrm{Ad}_{h}Y,g,X).

The maps are consistent with the potentials, that is
\hat{f}_{\Delta}^{*}(W)=W_{\Delta}=\hat{\pi}^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(W_{\mathrm{Dr}})
.
Both maps
\hat{f}_{\Delta}
and
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
have a clear geometric meaning.
The map
\hat{f}_{\Delta}
identifies
\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}
with a subvariety of
\mathscr{X}
corresponding to the diagonal
\mathfrak{n}_{\Delta}\subset\mathfrak{n}\times\mathfrak{n}
, which is invariant with respect to the action of the diagonal subgroup
B_{\Delta}\subset B^{2}
. As for
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
, note that
\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}/B_{\Delta}=\mathfrak{g}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times G
and the
B_{\Delta}
-equivariant map
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
descends on the quotient to
\mathfrak{g}\times\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathrm{Fl}\times G\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}\times\mathfrak{g}\times G
acting as the Springer resolution.

Now the Chern functor and its adjoint co-Chern functor are compositions of a pull-back and a push-forward:

\mathrm{CH}=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}*}\circ\hat{f}_{\Delta}^{*},\qquad\mathrm{HC}=\hat{f}_{\Delta*}\circ\hat{\pi}^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}.

From the computational perspective it is convenient to split
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
into a composition of two maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}}

where
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}=\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{b}
and the embedding
\iota
is generated by the natural inclusion
\mathfrak{n}_{\Delta}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{b}
combined with the adjoint action
\iota(X,g,Y,h)=(\textup{Ad}_{h}Y,g,X,h,Y)
. The map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}

is a projection on the first three factors.

Let us also point out that a formula for co-Chern functor in this section requires many clarifications and correction:
it is not immediately clear what is an adjoint functor to the restriction of
B^{2}
-equivariant structure to
the
B_{\Delta}
-equivariant structure. We spell out the omitted technical details in the next subsection where we also introduce maps
j^{0},f_{\Delta}
in the
commuting diagram:

(4.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}}

4.4.
The Chern functor: details

As we explained in the previous subsection, we need two auxiliary spaces in order to define the Chern functor:

\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}=\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{n},\quad\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}=\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathfrak{b}

The action of
G\times B
on these spaces is

(k,b)\cdot(Z,g,X,h,Y)=(\textup{Ad}_{k}(Z),\textup{Ad}_{k}(g),\textup{Ad}_{k}(X),khb,\textup{Ad}_{b^{-1}}(Y)).

The spaces
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{X}
are endowed with the standard
G\times B^{2}
-equivariant structure, the action of
B^{2}
on
\mathscr{C}
is trivial.
The following maps

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},\quad f_{\Delta}:\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{X},\quad j^{0}:\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\to\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}.

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}(Z,g,X,h,Y)=(Z,g,X),\quad f_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y)=(X,gh,Y,h,Y)

are fully equivariant if we restrict the
B^{2}
-equivariant structure on

\mathscr{X}
to the
B
-equivariant structure via the diagonal embedding

\Delta:B\rightarrow B^{2}
. We use notation

\mathrm{Res}^{B^{2}}_{B}:\mathrm{MF}\to\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2},\textup{GL}_{n}\times B_{\Delta}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},W),

for the corresponding restriction functor.
Note that
j^{0}
is the inclusion map.

Remark 4.4.1
.

A restriction functor between categories of weakly equivariant matrix factorizations needs to be carefully defined since the correction differentials
do not naturally restrict to correction differentials of a subgroup (see earlier version of this manuscript for the details of construction). On other hand
a construction of the restriction
functors for strictly equivariant matrix factorizations verbatim repeats the corresponding construction for the complexes of equivariant sheaves.
Thus we use equivalence (
4.7
) in our construction of
\mathrm{Res}^{B^{2}}_{B}
.

Using cyclic invariance of trace we derive:

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(W_{\mathrm{Dr}})-f^{*}_{\Delta}(W)=\textup{Tr}((\textup{Ad}_{g}^{-1}X-X)(\textup{Ad}_{h}Y-Z)).

Hence by discussion in section
2.1.3
there is a well-defined kernel of the Fourier-Mukai transform which is a Koszul matrix factorization

\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}:=[X-\textup{Ad}_{g^{-1}}X,\textup{Ad}_{h}Y-Z]\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}},\textup{GL}_{n}\times B_{\Delta}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(W_{\mathrm{Dr}})-f^{*}_{\Delta}(W)).

Remark 4.4.2
.

The subvariety
\mathcal{X}_{\Delta}
can be embedded inside
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}
by setting
\textup{Ad}_{h}Y=Z
, see diagram
4.10
.
Respectively, the restriction of
f_{\Delta}
on
\mathcal{X}_{\Delta}
is
\hat{f}_{\Delta}
.

Definition 4.4.3
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{MF}
we define value of Chern functor
\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by:

(4.11)

\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C}):=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}\otimes(j^{0}_{*}\circ f^{*}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{Res}^{B^{2}}_{B}(\mathcal{C}))))^{T}).

Remark 4.4.4
.

To lighten up notations we omit
\mathrm{Res}^{B^{2}}_{B}
in our computations and formulas.

Here and everywhere below we use notation
(-)^{T}
for
T
-invariants.
Since by our assumption (
4.5
) our matrix factorizations are graded
with respect to
T
-action, the functor
(-)^{T}
is just an extraction of the
T
-degree

0
part of the matrix factorization.

To define functor
\mathrm{HC}
we need to introduce a dual matrix factorization:

\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}:=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\vee}\in\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}},\textup{GL}_{n}\times B_{\Delta}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0},-\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(W_{\mathrm{Dr}})+f^{*}_{\Delta}(W)),

as well as the induction functor that is the left adjoint to the restriction functor
\mathrm{Res}^{B^{2}}_{B}
. Unfortunately, there is no readily available
left adjoint functor. In the next few paragraphs we construct a substitute for such functor.

The product
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B
has a
B\times B
-equivariant structure: for
(p,g)\in\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B
we define

(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(p,g)=(h_{1}\cdot p,h_{1}^{-1}gh_{2})

Then the following map is
B^{2}
-equivariant:

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}:\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{0}\times B\rightarrow\mathscr{X}\times B,

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y,b)=(X,gh,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,b).

The map
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}
is a composition of the projection along the first factor of
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}
and
the embedding inside
\mathscr{X}\times B
. The embedding is defined by the formula

\textup{Ad}_{b}Y_{1}=Y_{2},

so it is a regular embedding. Moreover, we have

j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}))\in\mathrm{MF}_{G\times B^{2}}(\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B,\tilde{f}_{\Delta}^{*}(W)),\quad\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\vee},

where
\tilde{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{HC}}\times B\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
is a natural extension of map

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by the projection along
B
. Thus
we have a well-defined matrix factorization

\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}))\in\mathrm{MF}_{G\times B^{2}}(\mathscr{X}\times B,\pi_{B}^{*}(W)),

where
\pi_{B}
is the projection along the last factor.

Definition 4.4.5
.

For
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
we define value of co-Chern functor
\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\in\mathrm{MF}
by:

(4.12)

\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D}):=\pi_{B*}(\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}))).

The functors used in the construction of the functors
\mathrm{HC}
and
\mathrm{CH}

are
G
-equivariant. On the other hand by construction of the functor
\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}
(
3.5
) the two periodic complexes

(4.13)

\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}(\mathcal{D},\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C})),\quad\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}(\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{C})

are complexes of
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{C}]^{G}
and
\mathbb{C}[\mathscr{X}]^{G\times B^{2}}
-modules, respectively.
Both spaces
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathscr{X}
have
\mathfrak{g}
as their first factor. The corresponding projections on
\mathfrak{g}
are
G
-equivariant and thus the
complexes (
4.13
) are naturally the complexes of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}
-modules.
The complexes (
4.13
) have the double grading that comes from the
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-action (
4.4
), (
4.3
), (
4.6
).

Proposition 4.4.6
.

The functor
\mathrm{HC}
is left adjoint to
\mathrm{CH}
, that is, for
any
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{MF}
and
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
we have
an isomorphism of doubly-graded two-periodic complexes
of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}
-modules:

\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}(\mathcal{D},\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C}))=\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}(\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{C}).

Proof.

By expanding the definition of
\mathcal{E}\mathrm{xt}
from (
3.5
)
taking into account the assumption (
4.5
) we derive that
the complexes from the main statement are:

\mathcal{D}^{\vee}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}\otimes j^{0}_{*}\circ f_{\Delta}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))^{T})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{2}}(\pi_{B*}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}))^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{C})^{T^{2}}.

Thus we have to compare these two objects of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g})
.
We simplify the second expression. Observe that the Chevalley-Eilenberg functor commutes with the
push-forward (
3.6
).

Since the first factor of
B
in
B^{2}
acts freely on
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B
, the functor

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{2}}(\dots)^{T^{2}}
is equivalent to the composition of the restriction to the
unity inside
B
and the functor
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\dots)^{T}
with respect to the diagonal action of
B
. In other words:

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{2}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{T^{2}}=\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{T},

for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.
When restricted to the unit inside
B
, the map
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}
becomes
f_{\Delta}
.
So now we have to compare

\mathcal{D}^{\vee}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}\otimes j^{0}_{*}\circ f_{\Delta}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))^{T})\quad\text{and}\quad f_{\Delta*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}})\otimes j^{0*}\circ{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee}))\otimes\mathcal{C})^{T}),

since
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D})^{\vee}=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})
and

\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}^{\vee}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}.

The pull-back functor
f^{*}_{\Delta}
is adjoint to the corresponding push-forward functors, hence we need to compare homology of complexes

\mathcal{D}^{\vee}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes j^{0}_{*}\circ f_{\Delta}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))^{T})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}})\otimes j^{0*}\circ{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})\otimes f^{*}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}))^{T}.

Since the map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is
\mathfrak{n}
-equivariant and
\mathcal{D}^{\vee}
has a trivial
\mathfrak{n}
-structure we need to match the homology of
the complexes:

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})\otimes\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes j^{0}_{*}\circ f_{\Delta}^{*}(\mathcal{C}))^{T})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}})\otimes j^{0*}\circ{\pi}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})\otimes f^{*}_{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}))^{T}.

By the projection formula from proposition
3.2.1
the first complex is

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}\bigg(j^{0}_{*}\bigg(j^{0*}\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})\otimes j^{0*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}})\otimes f_{\Delta}^{*}(\mathcal{C})\bigg)\bigg)^{T}.

As a final step we use that
j^{0}_{*}
commutes with the Chevalley-Eilenberg functor (
3.6
).

∎

Remark 4.4.7
.

Thus
\mathrm{CH}
is a right adjoint of
\mathrm{HC}
. On the other hand
\mathrm{CH}
is not a left adjoint of
\mathrm{HC}
because the duality functor does not commute with the functor

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(-))
.

4.5.
Properties

Recall that the category
\mathrm{MF}
has a convolution algebra structure defined with the auxiliary convolution space

\mathscr{X}_{cnv}:=\mathfrak{g}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})\times(G\times\mathfrak{n}).

This space has a unique
B^{3}
-equivariant structure such that the projection maps

(4.14)

\pi_{ij}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},g_{3},Y_{3})=(X,g_{i},Y_{i},g_{j},Y_{j}),

commute with the action of each
B
.

Definition 4.5.1
.

Given two elements
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{MF}
we define their convolution by

(4.15)

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}:=\pi_{13*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}}(\pi_{12}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\pi_{23}^{*}(\mathcal{G}))^{T^{(2)}}),

In the definition and everywhere below
\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
,
T^{(2)}
stand for the Lie algebra of the
unipotent radical and the reductive quotient of the second factor in
B^{3}
.

Similarly, we define a monoidal structure on
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
:

Definition 4.5.2
.

Given
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
we define their convolution by:

(4.16)

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}:=\pi_{3*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{G}))

where the maps
\pi_{i}
are the maps
\mathfrak{g}\times G^{2}\times\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
are

\pi_{1}(Z,g_{1},g_{2},X)=(Z,g_{1},X),\quad\pi_{2}(Z,g_{1},g_{2},X)=(\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}Z,g_{2},X),

\pi_{3}(Z,g_{1},g_{2},X)=(Z,g_{2}g_{1},X).

The functor
\mathrm{HC}
respects the convolution product.

Proposition 4.5.3
.

The functor
\mathrm{HC}
is monoidal.

Consider the embedding of the nilpotent cone
j^{0}\colon\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}
.
Let us define

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{C}^{0},G\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W_{\mathrm{Dr}}),\quad\mathscr{C}^{0}=\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathcal{N},

where
W_{\mathrm{Dr}}(Z,g,X)=\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{g}Z-Z)).

By restricting the maps
\pi_{i}
in (
4.16
) to the nilpotent locus we obtain a definition of the
monoidal structure on the category
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}
such that the pull-back functor:

j^{0*}:\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}\to\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}

is a monoidal functor.
Define an analog of the functor
\mathrm{HC}
for the nilpotent version of our category:

\mathrm{HC}^{0}:\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}\to\mathrm{MF},\quad\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}):=\pi_{B*}(\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}))).

Since the maps
j^{0}
and
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}
commute,
the previous functor is the composition of the new one with the pull-back:

\mathrm{HC}=\mathrm{HC}^{0}\circ j^{0*}.

The nilpotent cone
\mathcal{N}
is singular and we have to exercise some care when we work with the category
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}
. Luckily, in the proof below we apply pull-back
functors along smooth or regular map to the elements of
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{0}
.

Proof of proposition
4.5.3
.

By the previous remark, it is enough to show that the functor
\mathrm{HC}^{0}
is monoidal. To simplify notations we use
\mathrm{K}
for the Koszul matrix factorization

\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}
.
Our proof relies on the base change along the rectangle of maps:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{cnv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}\times\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0}_{cnv}\times G\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\circ\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{i}_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\times\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\circ\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{3}\times\pi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{C}}

where

\mathscr{Y}=\mathfrak{g}^{3}\times G^{3}\times\mathfrak{n}\times B

and the dotted maps will be explained below.
First of all, we explain why pushing along the solid arrows results in
\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\star\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})
. Indeed, expand
the expression for the convolution:

\pi_{13*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}}(i^{*}_{cnv}(\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ\pi_{B*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))\boxtimes\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ\pi_{B*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}))))^{T^{(2)}}),

where
i_{cnv}\colon\mathscr{X}_{cnv}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}\times\mathscr{X}
is the natural inclusion:

i_{cnv}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},g_{3},Y_{3})=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2})\times(X,g_{2},Y_{2},g_{3},Y_{3}).

Next we notice that
\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
and
T^{(2)}
act freely on
\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B\times\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B
and since
this space is a domain for
\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\boxtimes\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})
,
we replace the functor
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}}(\dots)^{T^{(2)}}
with the restriction functor
(\dots)_{b^{\prime}=1}
where

b^{\prime}
is an element of the first copy of
B
in
\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B\times\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B
. Thus the convolution is given by the pull-backs push-forwards
along the solid arrows of the above diagram:

\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\star\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})=\pi_{13*}(i^{*}_{cnv}(\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))\boxtimes\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ\pi_{B*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}))))).

Define the maps
\hat{i}_{cnv}
and
\hat{f}
:

(4.17)

\hat{i}_{cnv}(Z_{1},Z_{2},X,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},Y,b)=(Z_{1},g_{1}g_{2}^{-1},X,g_{2},Y,Z_{2},g_{2}bg_{3}^{-1},X,g_{3}b^{-1},Y),

\hat{f}(Z_{1},Z_{2},X,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},Y,b)=(X,g_{1},Y,g_{2},Y,g_{3},\textup{Ad}_{b}Y).

Thus using the base change in the upper left corner of the diagram we obtain

\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\star\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})=\pi_{13*}\circ\hat{f}_{*}\circ\hat{i}^{*}_{cnv}(\mathrm{K}_{1}\boxtimes\mathrm{K}_{2}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}\times\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}\times\pi_{B}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})),

where
\mathrm{K}_{i}
is the pull-back of the kernel
\mathrm{K}
along the projection on the
i
-th copy of

\mathscr{Z}^{0}
in the product
\mathscr{Z}^{0}\times\mathscr{Z}^{0}\times B
.

Next define the maps
i_{C}
and
j_{cnv}
:

i_{C}(Z,X,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime},Y,b,h)=(Z,\textup{Ad}_{g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}}Z,X,g^{\prime}h,h,(g^{\prime\prime})^{-1}hb,Y,b),

j_{cnv}(Z,X,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime},Y,b,h)=(X,Z,g^{\prime},X,\textup{Ad}_{g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}}Z,g^{\prime\prime}).

Now because of the explicit formula for the Koszul matrix factorization
\mathrm{K}_{1}
and
the construction of the push-forward
[
OR18c
]
we conclude that

\hat{i}^{*}_{cnv}(\mathrm{K}_{1}\boxtimes\mathrm{K}_{2}\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}\times\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}\times\pi_{B}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}))=i_{C*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes j^{*}_{cnv}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})),

where
\mathrm{K}=i_{C}^{*}(\mathrm{K}_{2})
. In more details, let use coordinates on
\mathscr{Y}
as in (
4.17
) to describe
the tensor product
\mathrm{K}_{1}\boxtimes\mathrm{K}_{2}
:

\displaystyle[\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}X-X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}Y-Z_{1}]

\displaystyle\otimes[\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g_{2}bg_{3}^{-1}}X-X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}Y-Z_{2}]=

\displaystyle[\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g_{1}}X-\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g_{2}}X,Y-\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}^{-1}Z_{1}]

\displaystyle\otimes[\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}^{-1}X-\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}^{-1}X,Y-\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}Z_{2}]=

\displaystyle[\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}^{-1}X-\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g_{2}}X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}Z_{2}-\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}^{-1}Z_{1}]

\displaystyle\otimes[\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}^{-1}X-\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}^{-1}X,Y-\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}Z_{2}]

The first equality is given by a change of basis in each Koszul matrix factorization.
The second follows from the row operation transformation of the matrix factorizations.

Thus the second term in the last tensor product is
\mathrm{K}
. The first factor is the matrix factorization that defines push-forward
i_{C*}
since the image of the
embedding
i_{C}
is defined by
\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}b^{-1}}Z_{2}-\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}^{-1}Z_{1}
, see iterative construction of push-forward in the proof of proposition
3.1.2
.

There is a unique map
\pi_{Y}
that makes our diagram commute. The commutativity of
the diagram implies the formula

\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\star\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})=\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\circ\pi_{B*}\circ\pi_{Y*}\circ i_{C*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes j^{*}_{cnv}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})).

Applying the base change to the lower-left corner in order to obtain another formula

\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\star\mathrm{HC}^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})=f_{\Delta*}\circ\pi_{B*}(\mathrm{K}\otimes\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times 1\circ\pi_{3*}\times\pi_{G*}\circ j_{cnv}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})),

where
\mathrm{K}
is
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{HC}}
. To complete proof we observe that

\pi_{3*}\times\pi_{G*}\circ j_{cnv}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime})=\pi_{B}^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\star\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}).

∎

Remark 4.5.4
.

Techniques of the previous proof can be used to show the
”projection” formula, as it was suggested to the authors by the anonymous referee:

\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C}\star\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D}))=\mathrm{CH}(\mathcal{C})\star\mathcal{D},

where
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{MF}
. A proof will appear in a subsequent publication.

Let us recall from
[
OR18c
]
that the unit in
(\mathrm{MF},\star)
is defined by

(4.18)

\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}=j_{\parallel*}(\mathcal{O}),

where

j_{\parallel}:\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathscr{X}(B)\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathscr{X}
,
\mathscr{X}(B)=\mathfrak{g}\times B\times G\times\mathfrak{n}

is defined by

j_{\parallel}(Z,g,X,b,k,Y)=(Z,g,X,k,Y,kb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y).

Proposition 4.5.5
.

For any
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
and a pair of Koszul matrix factorizations
\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{C}^{-}\in\mathrm{MF}
such that
\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathcal{C}^{-}\sim\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}
,
there is a homotopy equivalence:

(4.19)

\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\star\mathcal{C}^{-}\sim\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D}).

Proof.

The space
\hat{\mathscr{X}}=\mathfrak{g}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{4}\times B
has natural
B^{4}
-equivariant projections

\hat{\pi}_{ij}:\hat{\mathscr{X}}\to\mathscr{X}
as well as an embedding

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2}\times B\to\hat{\mathscr{X}}

defined as

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},gh,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,g_{4},Y_{4},b).

The double product in the statement is equal to
\hat{\pi}_{14*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\times\mathfrak{n}^{(3)}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})^{T^{(2)}\times T^{(3)}})
where
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is the complex on

\hat{\mathscr{X}}
:

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\hat{\pi}_{12}^{*}(\mathcal{C}^{+})\otimes\hat{\pi}_{34}^{*}(\mathcal{C}^{-})\otimes\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}\bigl(j^{0*}\circ\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{D})\otimes\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}\bigr).

Since the push-forward
\tilde{f}_{\Delta*}
is adjoint to the pull-back
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}^{*}
with respect to the
pairing
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\times\mathfrak{n}^{(3)}}(\cdot\otimes\cdot)^{T^{(2)}\times T^{(3)}}
, the l.h.s. of (
4.19
) has an expression

(4.20)

\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\star\mathcal{C}^{-}=\pi_{14*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\times\mathfrak{n}^{(3)}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime})^{T^{(2)}\times T^{(3)}}),

where
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
is the matrix factorization
on
\mathscr{Z}^{0}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2}\times B
:

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}:=\pi^{*}_{1\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{+})\otimes\pi^{*}_{\bullet 4}(\mathcal{C}^{-})\otimes j^{0*}\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D})\otimes\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}},

and the maps in the last formula are:

\pi_{1\bullet}(Z,g,X,h,Y_{\bullet},g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},gh,Y_{\bullet}),

\pi_{\bullet 4}(Z,g,X,h,Y_{\bullet},g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)=(X,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y_{\bullet},g_{4},Y_{4}),

\pi_{14}(Z,g,X,h,Y_{\bullet},g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4}).

These maps satisfy relations:
\hat{\pi}_{14}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{14}
,
\hat{\pi}_{12}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{1\bullet}
,

\hat{\pi}_{34}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{\bullet 4}
.

Just as in the proof of proposition
4.4.6
we observe that the left
B
-action on
B
is free, hence the functor

\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\times\mathfrak{n}^{(3)}}(\dots)^{T^{(2)}\times T^{(3)}}
is equivalent to the composition of the restriction to the unity in
B
and
\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\dots)^{T}
with respect to the diagonal action of
B
. In other words, (
4.20
) is equal to

\pi_{14*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}|_{b=1})^{T})
, here and everywhere below we use the same notations
\pi_{cd}
for the restriction of maps

\pi_{cd}
on the sub locus
b=1
.

Since
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}=[X-\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g}X,\textup{Ad}_{h}Y-Z]
, we can use row transformations of Koszul matrix factorizations in order to replace
X
with
\textup{Ad}_{g}X
in
\pi^{*}_{1\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{+})
. Now we combine this computation with the fact that the matrix
factorization
\pi^{*}_{1\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{+})
is strongly

G
-equivariant,
to establish the isomorphism

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}|_{b=1}\cong\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}:=\pi^{\prime*}_{1\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{+})\otimes\pi^{*}_{\bullet 4}(\mathcal{C}^{-})\otimes j^{0*}\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D})\otimes\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}},

where
\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}}=[X-\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g}X,*]
and the map
\pi^{\prime}_{1\bullet}
is

\pi^{\prime}_{1\bullet}(Z,g,X,h,Y_{\bullet},g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)=(X,g^{-1}g_{1},Y_{1},h,Y_{\bullet}).

To compute
*
in
\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}}
, observe that this matrix factorization has the potential:

\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{g^{-1}g_{1}}Y_{1}-\textup{Ad}_{h}Y_{\bullet}))+\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{h}Y_{\bullet}-\textup{Ad}_{g_{4}}Y_{4}))+\textup{Tr}(X(Z-\textup{Ad}_{g}Z))\\
-\textup{Tr}(X(\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}Y_{1}-\textup{Ad}_{g_{4}}Y_{4}))=\textup{Tr}((\textup{Ad}_{g}X-X)(\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}Y_{1}-Z))\\
=\textup{Tr}((X-\textup{Ad}_{g}^{-1}X)(\textup{Ad}_{g^{-1}g_{1}}Y_{1}-\textup{Ad}^{-1}_{g}Z))

Thus we conclude that
*=-\textup{Ad}_{g^{-1}g_{1}}Y_{1}+\textup{Ad}_{g}^{-1}Z
and in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}
only the first two factors depend on the variable
h,Y_{\bullet}
and have
a non-trivial
B
-action. Let
\pi
be the projection along
the coordinates
h,Y_{\bullet}
. Since
\pi_{*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}}(\pi^{\prime*}_{1\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{+})\otimes\pi^{*}_{\bullet 4}(\mathcal{C}^{-}))^{T})=\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathcal{C}^{-}\sim\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}

we have the homotopy between the convolution in (
4.19
)
and
\tilde{\pi}_{14*}(\tilde{\mathcal{C}})
, where

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}
is the matrix factorization on the space
\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathscr{X}
:

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}:=\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}\otimes j^{0*}\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D})\otimes\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}},

and
\tilde{\pi}_{14}
is determined by
\pi_{14}=\tilde{\pi}_{14}\circ\pi.

Now let us recall the formula (
4.18
) for
\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}
. Thus the convolution in (
4.19
)
is equal to
\pi_{B*}(j_{\parallel*}(j^{0*}\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(\mathcal{D})\otimes j_{\parallel}^{*}(\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}})))
, where
\pi_{B}
is the projection along
B
.
By identifying
\mathfrak{g}\times G\times\mathscr{X}(B)
with
\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{HC}}\times B
we match
j_{\parallel}
with
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}
.
Since
j^{*}_{\parallel}(\mathrm{K}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{CH}})=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}
the statement follows.
∎

In
[
OR18b
]
we constructed an monoidal functor from the affine braid group

\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}\colon\mathfrak{Br}_{\mathrm{aff}}\to\mathrm{MF}.

Under this homomorphism the generators of the group become Koszul matrix factorizations, thus the previous proposition
implies

Corollary 4.5.6
.

For any
\beta\in\mathfrak{Br}_{\mathrm{aff}}
and
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
we have:

\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\beta)\star\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\sim\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\star\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\beta).

Proof.

Let us present
\beta
as product of elementary braids
\beta=\sigma_{i_{1}}^{\epsilon_{1}}\cdot\sigma_{i_{\ell}}^{\epsilon_{\ell}}

The statement is equivalent to

\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\sigma_{i_{1}}^{\epsilon_{1}})\star\dots\star\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\sigma_{i_{\ell}}^{\epsilon_{\ell}})\star\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{D})\star\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\sigma_{i_{\ell}}^{-\epsilon_{1}})\star\dots\star\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\sigma_{i_{1}}^{\epsilon_{1}})\sim\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}.

Since the matrix factorization
\Phi^{\mathrm{aff}}(\sigma_{i}^{\epsilon})
is a Koszul matrix
factorization
[
OR18c
]
, we apply the previous proposition
\ell

times to prove the statement.
∎

Remark 4.5.7
.

The statement of proposition
4.5.5
holds without an assumption that
\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{C}^{-}
are Koszul matrix factorizations. To show that we need to use
systematically theorem 2.2 from
[
KR07
]
.

Since
W_{\mathrm{Dr}}(Z,g,X)=\textup{Tr}((g-1)[Zg^{-1},X])
, we have a Koszul matrix factorization

(4.21)

\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Dr}}=[g-1,[X,R]]\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}},\quad R=Zg^{-1}.

It is a unit (see proposition
4.5.8
) in the monoidal category
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.
The proof of theorem
1.0.2
requires a computation of the co-Chern of the matrix factorization
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.

Proposition 4.5.8
.

The element
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Dr}}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is the convolution unit and

\mathrm{HC}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Dr}})=\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}=j_{\parallel*}(\mathcal{O})
, where
j_{\parallel}\colon\mathscr{X}(B):=\mathfrak{g}\times B\times G\times\mathfrak{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}

is defined by

j_{\parallel}(X,b,h,Y)=(X,h,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y).

There is a unique
B^{2}
-equivariant structure on the space
\mathscr{X}(B)
that makes the map

j_{\parallel}
equivariant.
On the other hand,
\mathscr{X}(B)
embeds naturally into
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{HC}}\times B
:

(4.22)

i_{\mathrm{CH}}\colon\mathscr{X}(B)\to\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B,\quad i_{\mathrm{CH}}(X,b,h,Y)=(\textup{Ad}_{h}Y,1,X,h,Y,b).

Moreover,
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\circ i_{\mathrm{CH}}=j_{\parallel}
and the subvariety
i_{\mathrm{CH}}(\mathscr{X}(B))

is defined by equations

(4.23)

Z=\textup{Ad}_{h}Y,\quad g=1.

To complete the proof, observe that the Koszul matrix factorization
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Dr}})\otimes\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{CH}}
is the
Koszul matrix factorization for the ideal with the generators (
4.23
). The equations in (
4.23
) form a regular sequence, hence
by lemma 3.6 in
[
OR18c
]
(also see proposition
3.1.2
) such Koszul matrix factorization is unique and it coincides with the push-forward

i_{\mathrm{CH}*}(\mathcal{O})
by definition.

∎

5.
Coherent sheaves and Drinfeld center

In this section we introduce the framed and stable enhancements of the matrix factorizations from
\mathrm{MF}
and
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
:

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}},\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}},\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{st}},\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{Dr}},\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}.

The stable category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{st}}
appeared in
[
OR18c
]
, its convolution structure was used to represent the braid group
\mathfrak{Br}_{n}
and construct the link homology. The homology in
[
OR18c
]
are triply graded and categorify
the HOMFLYPT polynomial. Recently
[
OR20
]
, the authors have shown that the homology coincide with
the Khovanov-Rozansky homology
[
KR08a
]
, as it was conjectured in

[
GRN20
]
.
We recall this construction from
[
OR18c
]
in section
6
..

The framed stable categories
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}
,
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
appear naturally in the context of study of quiver varieties, framing is a standard
feature of Nakajima’s quiver varieties. The framed stable category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}
plays somewhat technical role in our constructions,
this category is essentially equivalent to the previously defined category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{st}}
, see remark
5.1.5
. The
framed stable category
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\mathrm{fs}}
on other hand is equivalent to the category of two-periodic complexes of coherent sheaves
on
\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
. Thus this stable framed category is essential for main result of the paper.

In more details, category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is related to the dg category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2}))
by the localization functor defined later:

\textup{loc}^{\mathrm{fs}}\colon\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2}))\rightarrow\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{Dr}},

where
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{per}}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2}))=\mathrm{MF}(\mathrm{Hilb}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2},\mathbb{T}_{q,t},tq^{0}))
is the derived category of two-periodic complexes of
\mathbb{T}_{q,t}
-equivariant coherent sheaves.

Moreover, we show that the functor
\textup{loc}^{\mathrm{fs}}
is an equivalence. Thus we have a well-defined functor

\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{loc}}:=\left(\textup{loc}^{\mathrm{fs}}\right)^{-1}\circ\mathrm{CH}^{fs}.

For various framing construction we use the free rank
n
bundles with various
G\times B^{2}
equivariant structures. Let us fix notations

\mathbb{C}^{n}=V_{H}\ni v,\quad h\cdot v=hv,

\mathbb{C}^{n}=V_{H}^{*}\ni v,\quad h\cdot v=vh^{-1},

where
H
can be
G,B^{(1)},B^{(2)},B^{(3)}
which are the factors of
G\times B^{3}
. Respectively, the other factors of
G\times B^{3}
act trivially on bundle.

We realize the space
V_{H}
as vector space of columns
\textup{Hom}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{n})
and

V^{*}_{H}
as vector space of rows
\textup{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C})
. Thus the products

vw
,
wv
,
v\in V_{H}
,
w\in V^{*}_{H}
are well-defined.

5.1.
Framed categories

Define framed versions of the spaces
\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{C}

\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=\mathscr{X}\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{B^{(1)}}\times V_{B^{(2)}},\quad\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=\mathscr{C}\times V_{G}^{*}\times V_{G},

and their potentials

W^{\mathrm{fr}}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},w,v_{1},v_{2}):=W(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2})+\textup{Tr}(w(g_{1}v_{1}-g_{2}v_{2})),

W^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}(X,g,Z,w,v)=W_{\mathrm{Dr}}(X,g,Z)+\textup{Tr}(w(v-gv)).

Definition 5.1.1
.

We define the framed categories as

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}},G\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W^{\mathrm{fr}}),\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{fr}},G\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}).

The framed categories have a natural monoidal structure similar to the unframed case.
The convolution spaces are

\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{cnv}}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{cnv}}\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{B^{(1)}}\times V_{B^{(2)}}\times V_{B^{(3)}},\quad\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{cnv}}:=\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{cnv}}\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{G}

and the extended maps are

\pi_{ij}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},g_{3},Y_{3},w,v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})=(X,g_{i},Y_{i},g_{j},Y_{j},w,v_{i},v_{j}),

\pi_{1}(Z,g_{1},g_{2},X,w,v)=(Z,g_{1},X,w,v),\quad\pi_{2}(Z,g_{1},g_{2},X,w,v)=(Z,g_{2},\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}X,w,g_{1}v),

\pi_{3}(Z,X,g_{1},g_{2},w,v)=(Z,g_{1}g_{2},X,w,v).

The convolution product is defined by the formulas (
4.15
) and (
4.16
).

Definition 5.1.2
.

Let us define

\mathrm{MF}^{v}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{v},G\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W),\quad\mathscr{X}^{v}=\mathscr{X}\times V_{G}.

here we use the natural projection
\mathscr{X}^{v}\to\mathscr{X}
to define
W
on
\mathscr{X}^{v}
.

This category plays an auxiliary role but it naturally related to the stable
categories discussed in the next section.

Proposition 5.1.3
.

The category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is equivalent to the category
\mathrm{MF}^{v}
.

Proof.

The equivalence
i_{\mathrm{fr}}\colon\mathrm{MF}^{v}\to\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}

is defined as

i_{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{F}):=\pi^{*}_{V^{*}V}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}},\quad\pi_{V^{*}V}:\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}\to\mathscr{X}

where
\pi_{V^{*}V}
is a projection along the factors
V_{G}^{*}
and
V_{B^{(2)}}
:

\pi_{V^{*}V}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},w,v_{1},v_{2})=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},g_{1}v_{1})

while the Koszul matrix factorization on
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}
on is

\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=[w,g_{1}v_{1}-g_{2}v_{2}]\in\mathrm{MF}_{G\times B^{2}}^{\mathbb{T}_{q,t}}(\mathscr{X},\textup{Tr}(w(g_{1}v_{1}-g_{2}v_{2}))),

The inverse functor is defined in terms of embedding:

i_{w=0}:\mathscr{X}\times V_{B^{(1)}}\times V_{B^{(2)}}\to\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}},\quad i_{w=0}(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},v_{1},v_{2})=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{2},Y_{2},v_{1},v_{2},0)

i^{-1}_{\mathrm{fr}}=\pi_{V*}\circ i_{w=0}^{*},

here
\pi_{V}
is the projection along
V_{B^{(2)}}
.

∎

The equivalence between
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}
and
\mathrm{MF}^{v}
is an example of Knörrer periodicity equivalence
3.6.1
. In particular, one can see that
i_{\mathrm{fr}}=i_{w=0,*}\circ\pi^{*}_{V}
. The category
\mathrm{MF}^{v}
has a monoidal structure defined by

(5.1)

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{G}:=(\pi_{13}\times 1)_{*}(\mathrm{CE}_{\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}}(\pi^{*}_{12}\times 1^{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\pi^{*}_{23}\times 1^{*}(\mathcal{G}))^{T^{(2)}}),

here
\pi_{ij}
are the maps (
4.14
).

Proposition 5.1.4
.

The functor
i_{\mathrm{fr}}
is a monoidal equivalence.

Proof.

First we observe that with use of row transformation of Koszul matrix factorizations (see for example
[
OR18c
, section 2.3]
) one can obtain the equality of Koszul matrix factorizations

\pi_{12}^{*}(\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}})\otimes\pi_{23}^{*}(\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}})=\pi_{12}^{*}([w,g_{1}v_{1}-g_{2}v_{2}])\otimes\pi^{*}_{23}([w,g_{2}v_{2}-g_{3}v_{3}])\\
=[0,g_{2}v_{2}]\otimes[w,g_{1}v_{1}-g_{3}v_{3}]=[0,g_{2}v_{2}]\otimes\pi^{*}_{13}(\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}})

Thus
\pi_{12}^{*}(i^{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{F}))\otimes\pi_{23}^{*}(i^{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{G}))
is homotopy equivalent to the restriction of
\pi_{12}^{*}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\pi_{23}^{*}(\mathcal{G})\otimes\pi_{13}^{*}(\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fr}})

to the locus
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{cnv}}^{\mathrm{fr}}|_{v_{2}=0}
. Thus the statement follows from the projection formula for
\pi_{13}
.
∎

The monoidal functor from above sends the unit
\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}

to the unit element
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\parallel}
in the category
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.
Let us spell out the construction of the last unit. Indeed we have

\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}^{\mathrm{fr}}=j_{\parallel*}^{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{O})
, where

j_{\parallel}^{\mathrm{fr}}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}(B):=\mathfrak{g}\times B\times G\times\mathfrak{n}\times V_{G}^{*}\times V_{G}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}\times V_{G}^{*}\times V_{G}

is defined by

j_{\parallel}(X,b,h,Y,w,v)=(X,h,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,h^{-1}(v),b^{-1}h^{-1}(v)).

There is a unique
B^{2}
-equivariant structure on the space
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}(B)
that makes the map

j_{\parallel}^{\mathrm{fr}}
equivariant.

Remark 5.1.5
.

It seems to be natural to define
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{v}
as category of
matrix factorizations on
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{v}\subset\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times V_{G}
defined by
the condition
v=gv
and the potential defined by restricting

W_{\mathrm{Dr}}
. So one can expect an intimate relation between
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{v}
and

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\mathrm{fr}}
. However, we choose to avoid working with this category since

\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{v}
is singular and we need to use some complicated tools to work with
this category.

5.2.
Framed Chern and co-Chern functors

The framed categories are connected by the framed versions of the functors
\mathrm{HC}
and
\mathrm{CH}
. The framed version of the
space
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}
and maps
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}
,
f_{\Delta}
are defined as

\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{fr}}:=\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{G},\quad\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{fr}}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fr}},\quad f_{\Delta}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{fr}}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}(Z,g,X,h,Y,w,v)=(Z,g,X,w,v),

f_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y,w,v)=(X,gh,Y,h,Y,w,h^{-1}g^{-1}(v),h^{-1}(v)).

We define the framed version of the Chern character functor
\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{fr}}
by the formula (
4.11
).
The framed version
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is defined by (
4.12
), where this time

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\colon\mathscr{Z}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B\rightarrow\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times B,

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y,w,v,b)=(X,gh,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,h^{-1}(v),b^{-1}h^{-1}g^{-1}(v),w,b).

Similarly, we define
\mathscr{Z}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}=\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times V_{G}^{*}\times V_{G}
and
j^{0}:\mathscr{Z}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}\to\mathscr{Z}^{0}_{\mathrm{CH}}
is a natural embedding.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Functors from the diagram below

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

have the following properties

•

The functor
\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is a right adjoint of
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fr}}
,

•

The functor
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is monoidal.

•

For any
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\mathrm{fr}}
and a pair of Koszul matrix factorizations
\mathcal{C}^{+},\mathcal{C}^{-}\in\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fr}}
such that
\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathcal{C}^{-}\sim\mathcal{C}_{\parallel}^{\mathrm{fr}}
,
there is a homotopy equivalence:

\mathcal{C}^{+}\star\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{D})\star\mathcal{C}^{-}\sim\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fr}}(\mathcal{D}).

Proof.

Proof of the first statement word by word repeats the proof of proposition
4.4.6
. For second statement we can use the argument from proof of
proposition
4.5.3
. We just need to use a framed version of the commuting diagram of maps of spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{cnv}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Z}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times\mathscr{Z}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}_{cnv}\times G\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathscr{C}^{0,\mathrm{fr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\circ\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{i}_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\times\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\circ\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}\times\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{3}\times\pi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{cnv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{C}}

where
\mathscr{Y}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\mathscr{Y}\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{G}
and framed
versions of the corresponding maps that start at
\mathscr{Y}^{\mathrm{fr}}
are:

\hat{i}_{cnv}(Z_{1},Z_{2},X,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},Y,b,w,v)=(Z_{1},g_{1}g_{2}^{-1},X,g_{2},Y,w,v,Z_{2},g_{2}bg_{3}^{-1},X,g_{3}b^{-1},Y,w,v),

\hat{f}(Z_{1},Z_{2},X,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},Y,b,w,v)=(X,g_{1},Y,g_{2},Y,g_{3},\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,w,g_{1}^{-1}(v),g_{2}^{-1}(v),bg_{3}^{-1}(v)).

Respectively, the framing alteration of the other map is more straight forward:

i_{C}(Z,X,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime},Y,w,v,b,h)=(Z,\textup{Ad}_{g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}}Z,X,g^{\prime}h,h,(g^{\prime\prime})^{-1}hb,Y,b,w,v),

j_{cnv}(Z,X,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime},Y,w,v,b,h)=(X,Z,g^{\prime},w,v,X,\textup{Ad}_{g^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}}Z,g^{\prime\prime},w,v).

With the last modifications of the proof of proposition
4.5.3
yields
the second statement of the current proposition.

For a proof of the third statement we use the argument of proposition
4.5.5
. We need to work with the framed versions of
the spaces and maps. In particular, the framed space

\hat{\mathscr{X}}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\mathfrak{g}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{4}\times B\times V^{*}_{G}\times V_{B^{(1)}}\times V_{B^{(2)}}\times V_{B^{(3)}}\times V_{B^{(4)}}

has natural
B^{4}
-equivariant projections

\hat{\pi}_{ij}:\hat{\mathscr{X}}^{\mathrm{fr}}\to\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}
as well as an embedding
\tilde{f}_{\Delta}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times(G\times\mathfrak{n})^{2}\times B

defined as

\tilde{f}_{\Delta}(Z,g,X,h,Y,w,v,g_{1},Y_{1},g_{4},Y_{4},b)\\
=(X,g_{1},Y_{1},gh,Y,hb,\textup{Ad}_{b}Y,g_{4},Y_{4},b,w,g_{1}^{-1}(v),h^{-1}g^{-1}(v),b^{-1}h^{-1}(v),g_{4}(v)).

The other maps used in the proof are defined by the relations:
\hat{\pi}_{14}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{14}
,
\hat{\pi}_{12}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{1\bullet}
,

\hat{\pi}_{34}\circ\tilde{f}_{\Delta}=\pi_{\bullet 4}
.

With these modifications proof of proposition
4.5.5
yields the
third statement of the current proposition.
∎

5.3.
Stable and framed stable categories

The stable versions of the categories are defined on the stable pieces of the corresponding varieties. In particular, we have
embeddings:

j_{\mathrm{st}}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}\times V_{G}=\mathscr{X}^{v},\quad\quad j_{\mathrm{fs}}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fs}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}},\quad\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fs}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fr}}.

Denote by
v
the coordinate along the vector space
V
. The open stability condition for
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}}
and
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fs}}
is defined by the following requirements:

(5.2)

\mathbb{C}\langle X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}^{-1}Y_{1}\rangle\,v=V_{G},\quad\mathbb{C}\langle X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}^{-1}Y_{1}\rangle\,g^{-1}_{1}(v_{1})=V_{G},

where
v_{1}
denotes coordinate along the factor
V_{B^{(1)}}
in
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.
The open stability condition for
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fs}}
is

\mathbb{C}\langle Z,X\rangle\,v=V_{G}.

The stable piece
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{cnv}}\subset\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{cnv}}\times V
of the convolution space is an open subset
which is an intersection

\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{cnv}}:=(\pi_{12}\times 1)^{-1}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}})\cap(\pi_{23}\times 1)^{-1}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}}).

Definition 5.3.1
.

We define stable categories

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{st}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{st}},G\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W),\quad\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{Dr}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fs}},G\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W^{\mathrm{fr}}),

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}:=\mathrm{MF}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fs}},G\times B^{2}\times\mathbb{T}_{q,t},q^{0}t,W^{\mathrm{fr}})

We define the convolution product on
\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{st}}
by the formula (
5.1
).
The monoidal structure is defined on categories

\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}_{\mathrm{Dr}},\mathrm{MF}^{\mathrm{fs}}

by restricting the corresponding maps to the stable pieces of the convolution spaces.

The same argument as in Theorem 1.2.3 of
[
OR18b
]
implies the following:

Proposition 5.3.2
.

The pull-back maps
j_{\mathrm{fs}}^{*}
,
j_{\mathrm{st}}^{*}\circ\pi^{*}_{V}
, where
\pi_{V}
is the projection along
V
, are monoidal functors.

Proof.

The statement that
j^{*}_{\mathrm{st}}\circ\pi_{V}^{*}
is a monoidal functor is shown in
Theorem 1.2.3 of
[
OR18c
]
. The same method yields the statement
for
j_{\mathrm{fs}}^{*}
, we remind the details below. The key observation is the
shrinking lemma from
[
OR18c
]
(see lemma 12.3 from
[
OR18c
]
). The lemma states that the pull back to an open Zarisky subset is an equivalence of the categories
of matrix factorizations as long as the open set contains all critical points
of the potential.

That is the category of matrix factorizations only depends on the formal neighborhood of the critical locus. Thus we
need to study the stable locus of the intersection of two critical loci
Crit(\pi_{12}^{*}(W^{\mathrm{fr}}))
and
Crit(\pi_{23}^{*}(W^{\mathrm{fr}}))
inside
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.

But on this intersection
\textup{Ad}_{g_{1}}^{-1}Y=\textup{Ad}_{g_{2}}^{-1}Y=\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}}^{-1}Y
. Hence
the stable condition can be imposed by requiring

\mathbb{C}\langle X,\textup{Ad}_{g_{3}}^{-1}Y\rangle v=V_{G}.

This open condition is constant along the fibers
of projection
\pi_{13}
hence this condition does not interfere with the convolution and the statement follows.

∎

5.4.
Framed stable Chern and co-Chern functor

We define the framed stable version of the space
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}
as an open locus inside
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}\times V
defined as an intersection:

\mathscr{Z}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{fs}}:=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{-1}(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{fs}})\cap f_{\Delta}^{-1}(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{fs}}),

where the corresponding maps
f_{\Delta}
,
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr}}
are defined by restricting the framed maps to the locus
where the covectors vanish. Thus we can define the functors
\mathrm{CH}^{\mathrm{fs}}
and
\mathrm{HC}^{\mathrm{fs}}
by the formulas (
4.11
)
and (
4.12
). Moreover, the functors constructed in this section fit into the diagram

(5.3)
